### 1. The Core Principle: Additive Independence

> **Section:** §20 Greedy Intermediate (Slope Trick, WQS)  
> **Phase:** Phase 5 — Dynamic Programming & Advanced Greedy  
> **Contest ROI:** B-tier | **Rating Range:** 1600–2200 | **Frequency:** Common  

---

#### 2. Theoretical Deep-Dive & Mathematical Mechanics

##### Overview

Intermediate Greedy techniques bridge convex optimization and dynamic programming: Decoupled Dimension Sweeps, Slope Trick (piecewise linear convex function maintenance via priority queues), and WQS Binary Search (Alien's Trick).

##### Fundamental Theorems & Invariants

- **Additive Independence & Chebyshev Transform:** In $\ell_1$ (Manhattan) and $\ell_\infty$ (Chebyshev) metrics, coordinate transformations $(x', y') = (x+y, x-y)$ decouple multi-dimensional distance minimization into independent 1D median problems.
- **Slope Trick Invariant:** Any continuous piecewise linear convex function $f(x)$ with integer slopes can be represented by its minimum value and two priority queues storing the inflection points where the slope changes by $+1$ and $-1$. Adding $|x - a|$ updates slope inflection points in $\mathcal{O}(\log N)$.
- **Alien's Trick / WQS Binary Search:** If the optimal value $OPT(k)$ as a function of choice count $k$ is strictly concave/convex, introducing a Lagrange multiplier penalty $\lambda$ removes the exact-count constraint, binary searching $\lambda$ in $\mathcal{O}(\text{BaseDP} \cdot \log(\text{Range})))$.

##### Algorithmic Execution Blueprint

1. **Chebyshev Transformation:** Map each point $(x, y) \to (x+y, x-y)$. The maximum Chebyshev distance $\max(|x_1-x_2|, |y_1-y_2|)$ becomes $\frac{1}{2}(|x'_1-x'_2| + |y'_1-y'_2|)$.
2. **Slope Trick Maintenance:** Maintain left heap $L$ (max-heap) and right heap $R$ (min-heap). To add $|x - a|$: if $a < L.\text{top}()$, add cost $L.\text{top}() - a$, push $a$ twice to $L$, and move $L.\text{top}()$ to $R$.
3. **WQS Binary Search:** Binary search penalty $\lambda$. Run unconstrained DP where each selection costs $+\lambda$. If optimal selections count $> k$, increase $\lambda$.

##### Closed-Form Invariant Equations

$$f_{new}(x) = f(x) + |x - a|, \quad \text{WQS}: \min_k \big(dp[k] - \lambda k\big) \iff \text{Lagrangian Duality}$$

##### Structural Edge Cases & Assumptions

- Slope Trick requires the underlying objective function to be strictly convex; non-convex cost functions require general CHT or DP.
- In WQS Binary Search, collinear points (flat segments in the convex hull of $OPT(k)$) require careful tie-breaking (preferring max count or min count) to avoid binary search oscillation.

---

#### 5. Contest-Ready Implementation Templates

##### Python 3 Template

```python
import sys

def solve():
    # points = [(x1, y1), (x2, y2), ...]
    xs = sorted([p[0] for p in points])
    ys = sorted([p[1] for p in points])
    
    n = len(xs)
    
    # Decoupled Greedy Choice: 
    # The optimal X is the median of xs, optimal Y is the median of ys.
    target_x = xs[n // 2]
    target_y = ys[n // 2]
    
    total_dist = 0
    for x in xs:
        total_dist += abs(x - target_x)
    for y in ys:
        total_dist += abs(y - target_y)
        
    return total_dist
```

<a id="topic-6"></a>
### 6. The Median Invariant: Why it Minimizes Absolute Cost

> **Section:** §20 Greedy Intermediate (Slope Trick, WQS)  
> **Phase:** Phase 5 — Dynamic Programming & Advanced Greedy  
> **Contest ROI:** B-tier | **Rating Range:** 1600–2200 | **Frequency:** Common  

---

#### 2. Theoretical Deep-Dive & Mathematical Mechanics

##### Overview

Intermediate Greedy techniques bridge convex optimization and dynamic programming: Decoupled Dimension Sweeps, Slope Trick (piecewise linear convex function maintenance via priority queues), and WQS Binary Search (Alien's Trick).

##### Fundamental Theorems & Invariants

- **Additive Independence & Chebyshev Transform:** In $\ell_1$ (Manhattan) and $\ell_\infty$ (Chebyshev) metrics, coordinate transformations $(x', y') = (x+y, x-y)$ decouple multi-dimensional distance minimization into independent 1D median problems.
- **Slope Trick Invariant:** Any continuous piecewise linear convex function $f(x)$ with integer slopes can be represented by its minimum value and two priority queues storing the inflection points where the slope changes by $+1$ and $-1$. Adding $|x - a|$ updates slope inflection points in $\mathcal{O}(\log N)$.
- **Alien's Trick / WQS Binary Search:** If the optimal value $OPT(k)$ as a function of choice count $k$ is strictly concave/convex, introducing a Lagrange multiplier penalty $\lambda$ removes the exact-count constraint, binary searching $\lambda$ in $\mathcal{O}(\text{BaseDP} \cdot \log(\text{Range})))$.

##### Algorithmic Execution Blueprint

1. **Chebyshev Transformation:** Map each point $(x, y) \to (x+y, x-y)$. The maximum Chebyshev distance $\max(|x_1-x_2|, |y_1-y_2|)$ becomes $\frac{1}{2}(|x'_1-x'_2| + |y'_1-y'_2|)$.
2. **Slope Trick Maintenance:** Maintain left heap $L$ (max-heap) and right heap $R$ (min-heap). To add $|x - a|$: if $a < L.\text{top}()$, add cost $L.\text{top}() - a$, push $a$ twice to $L$, and move $L.\text{top}()$ to $R$.
3. **WQS Binary Search:** Binary search penalty $\lambda$. Run unconstrained DP where each selection costs $+\lambda$. If optimal selections count $> k$, increase $\lambda$.

##### Closed-Form Invariant Equations

$$f_{new}(x) = f(x) + |x - a|, \quad \text{WQS}: \min_k \big(dp[k] - \lambda k\big) \iff \text{Lagrangian Duality}$$

##### Structural Edge Cases & Assumptions

- Slope Trick requires the underlying objective function to be strictly convex; non-convex cost functions require general CHT or DP.
- In WQS Binary Search, collinear points (flat segments in the convex hull of $OPT(k)$) require careful tie-breaking (preferring max count or min count) to avoid binary search oscillation.

#### 5. Contest-Ready Implementation Templates

##### Python 3 Template

```python
import sys

def solve_stick_lengths():
    n = int(sys.stdin.readline())
    p = list(map(int, sys.stdin.readline().split()))
    p.sort()
    
    median = p[n // 2]
    cost = sum(abs(x - median) for x in p)
    print(cost)
```

<a id="topic-13"></a>
### 13. Python Implementation: The Master Toolkit

> **Section:** §20 Greedy Intermediate (Slope Trick, WQS)  
> **Phase:** Phase 5 — Dynamic Programming & Advanced Greedy  
> **Contest ROI:** B-tier | **Rating Range:** 1600–2200 | **Frequency:** Common  

---

#### 2. Theoretical Deep-Dive & Mathematical Mechanics

##### Overview

Intermediate Greedy techniques bridge convex optimization and dynamic programming: Decoupled Dimension Sweeps, Slope Trick (piecewise linear convex function maintenance via priority queues), and WQS Binary Search (Alien's Trick).

##### Fundamental Theorems & Invariants

- **Additive Independence & Chebyshev Transform:** In $\ell_1$ (Manhattan) and $\ell_\infty$ (Chebyshev) metrics, coordinate transformations $(x', y') = (x+y, x-y)$ decouple multi-dimensional distance minimization into independent 1D median problems.
- **Slope Trick Invariant:** Any continuous piecewise linear convex function $f(x)$ with integer slopes can be represented by its minimum value and two priority queues storing the inflection points where the slope changes by $+1$ and $-1$. Adding $|x - a|$ updates slope inflection points in $\mathcal{O}(\log N)$.
- **Alien's Trick / WQS Binary Search:** If the optimal value $OPT(k)$ as a function of choice count $k$ is strictly concave/convex, introducing a Lagrange multiplier penalty $\lambda$ removes the exact-count constraint, binary searching $\lambda$ in $\mathcal{O}(\text{BaseDP} \cdot \log(\text{Range})))$.

##### Algorithmic Execution Blueprint

1. **Chebyshev Transformation:** Map each point $(x, y) \to (x+y, x-y)$. The maximum Chebyshev distance $\max(|x_1-x_2|, |y_1-y_2|)$ becomes $\frac{1}{2}(|x'_1-x'_2| + |y'_1-y'_2|)$.
2. **Slope Trick Maintenance:** Maintain left heap $L$ (max-heap) and right heap $R$ (min-heap). To add $|x - a|$: if $a < L.\text{top}()$, add cost $L.\text{top}() - a$, push $a$ twice to $L$, and move $L.\text{top}()$ to $R$.
3. **WQS Binary Search:** Binary search penalty $\lambda$. Run unconstrained DP where each selection costs $+\lambda$. If optimal selections count $> k$, increase $\lambda$.

##### Closed-Form Invariant Equations

$$f_{new}(x) = f(x) + |x - a|, \quad \text{WQS}: \min_k \big(dp[k] - \lambda k\big) \iff \text{Lagrangian Duality}$$

##### Structural Edge Cases & Assumptions

- Slope Trick requires the underlying objective function to be strictly convex; non-convex cost functions require general CHT or DP.
- In WQS Binary Search, collinear points (flat segments in the convex hull of $OPT(k)$) require careful tie-breaking (preferring max count or min count) to avoid binary search oscillation.

---

#### 5. Contest-Ready Implementation Templates

##### Python 3 Template

```python
import sys
from itertools import groupby
from math import gcd

def solve():
    n = int(sys.stdin.readline())
    a = list(map(int, sys.stdin.readline().split()))
    
    if n == 1: return 0
    
    pref = [0] * n
    suff = [0] * n
    
    pref[0] = a[0]
    for i in range(1, n):
        pref[i] = gcd(pref[i-1], a[i])
        
    suff[n-1] = a[n-1]
    for i in range(n-2, -1, -1):
        suff[i] = gcd(suff[i+1], a[i])
        
    # Greedy: Remove index i
    max_ans = max(suff[1], pref[n-2])
    for i in range(1, n-1):
        max_ans = max(max_ans, gcd(pref[i-1], suff[i+1]))
    
    return max_ans

def block_compress_example(s):
    # s = "aaabbccccd" -> [('a', 3), ('b', 2), ('c', 4), ('d', 1)]
    compressed = [(k, len(list(g))) for k, g in groupby(s)]
    return compressed
```

#### 5. Contest-Ready Implementation Templates

##### Python 3 Template

```python
import sys

def solve():
    def check(penalty):
        # Solves unconstrained DP with item cost 'penalty'
        # Returns: (max_value, count_of_items_used)
        dp = [0] * (n + 1)
        cnt = [0] * (n + 1)
        # ... standard unconstrained transitions ...
        return dp[n], cnt[n]

    low = 0
    high = 10**14
    best_lambda = 0
    
    for _ in range(100):
        mid = (low + high) / 2
        _, count = check(mid)
        if count >= k:
            best_lambda = mid
            low = mid
        else:
            high = mid
            
    val, count = check(best_lambda)
    # The true answer for K is: val + (best_lambda * K)
    print(val + best_lambda * k)
```

### 19. The Representation: Two Heaps

> **Section:** §20 Greedy Intermediate (Slope Trick, WQS)  
> **Phase:** Phase 5 — Dynamic Programming & Advanced Greedy  
> **Contest ROI:** B-tier | **Rating Range:** 1600–2200 | **Frequency:** Common  

---

#### 2. Theoretical Deep-Dive & Mathematical Mechanics

##### Overview

Intermediate Greedy techniques bridge convex optimization and dynamic programming: Decoupled Dimension Sweeps, Slope Trick (piecewise linear convex function maintenance via priority queues), and WQS Binary Search (Alien's Trick).

##### Fundamental Theorems & Invariants

- **Additive Independence & Chebyshev Transform:** In $\ell_1$ (Manhattan) and $\ell_\infty$ (Chebyshev) metrics, coordinate transformations $(x', y') = (x+y, x-y)$ decouple multi-dimensional distance minimization into independent 1D median problems.
- **Slope Trick Invariant:** Any continuous piecewise linear convex function $f(x)$ with integer slopes can be represented by its minimum value and two priority queues storing the inflection points where the slope changes by $+1$ and $-1$. Adding $|x - a|$ updates slope inflection points in $\mathcal{O}(\log N)$.
- **Alien's Trick / WQS Binary Search:** If the optimal value $OPT(k)$ as a function of choice count $k$ is strictly concave/convex, introducing a Lagrange multiplier penalty $\lambda$ removes the exact-count constraint, binary searching $\lambda$ in $\mathcal{O}(\text{BaseDP} \cdot \log(\text{Range})))$.

##### Algorithmic Execution Blueprint

1. **Chebyshev Transformation:** Map each point $(x, y) \to (x+y, x-y)$. The maximum Chebyshev distance $\max(|x_1-x_2|, |y_1-y_2|)$ becomes $\frac{1}{2}(|x'_1-x'_2| + |y'_1-y'_2|)$.
2. **Slope Trick Maintenance:** Maintain left heap $L$ (max-heap) and right heap $R$ (min-heap). To add $|x - a|$: if $a < L.\text{top}()$, add cost $L.\text{top}() - a$, push $a$ twice to $L$, and move $L.\text{top}()$ to $R$.
3. **WQS Binary Search:** Binary search penalty $\lambda$. Run unconstrained DP where each selection costs $+\lambda$. If optimal selections count $> k$, increase $\lambda$.

##### Closed-Form Invariant Equations

$$f_{new}(x) = f(x) + |x - a|, \quad \text{WQS}: \min_k \big(dp[k] - \lambda k\big) \iff \text{Lagrangian Duality}$$

##### Structural Edge Cases & Assumptions

- Slope Trick requires the underlying objective function to be strictly convex; non-convex cost functions require general CHT or DP.
- In WQS Binary Search, collinear points (flat segments in the convex hull of $OPT(k)$) require careful tie-breaking (preferring max count or min count) to avoid binary search oscillation.

#### 2. Theoretical Deep-Dive & Mathematical Mechanics

##### Overview

Intermediate Greedy techniques bridge convex optimization and dynamic programming: Decoupled Dimension Sweeps, Slope Trick (piecewise linear convex function maintenance via priority queues), and WQS Binary Search (Alien's Trick).

##### Fundamental Theorems & Invariants

- **Additive Independence & Chebyshev Transform:** In $\ell_1$ (Manhattan) and $\ell_\infty$ (Chebyshev) metrics, coordinate transformations $(x', y') = (x+y, x-y)$ decouple multi-dimensional distance minimization into independent 1D median problems.
- **Slope Trick Invariant:** Any continuous piecewise linear convex function $f(x)$ with integer slopes can be represented by its minimum value and two priority queues storing the inflection points where the slope changes by $+1$ and $-1$. Adding $|x - a|$ updates slope inflection points in $\mathcal{O}(\log N)$.
- **Alien's Trick / WQS Binary Search:** If the optimal value $OPT(k)$ as a function of choice count $k$ is strictly concave/convex, introducing a Lagrange multiplier penalty $\lambda$ removes the exact-count constraint, binary searching $\lambda$ in $\mathcal{O}(\text{BaseDP} \cdot \log(\text{Range})))$.

##### Algorithmic Execution Blueprint

1. **Chebyshev Transformation:** Map each point $(x, y) \to (x+y, x-y)$. The maximum Chebyshev distance $\max(|x_1-x_2|, |y_1-y_2|)$ becomes $\frac{1}{2}(|x'_1-x'_2| + |y'_1-y'_2|)$.
2. **Slope Trick Maintenance:** Maintain left heap $L$ (max-heap) and right heap $R$ (min-heap). To add $|x - a|$: if $a < L.\text{top}()$, add cost $L.\text{top}() - a$, push $a$ twice to $L$, and move $L.\text{top}()$ to $R$.
3. **WQS Binary Search:** Binary search penalty $\lambda$. Run unconstrained DP where each selection costs $+\lambda$. If optimal selections count $> k$, increase $\lambda$.

##### Closed-Form Invariant Equations

$$f_{new}(x) = f(x) + |x - a|, \quad \text{WQS}: \min_k \big(dp[k] - \lambda k\big) \iff \text{Lagrangian Duality}$$

##### Structural Edge Cases & Assumptions

- Slope Trick requires the underlying objective function to be strictly convex; non-convex cost functions require general CHT or DP.

---

#### 5. Contest-Ready Implementation Templates

##### Python 3 Template

```python
import heapq

class SlopeTrick:
    def __init__(self):
        self.L = [] # Max-heap (-val)
        self.R = [] # Min-heap (+val)
        self.min_f = 0
        self.off_L = 0
        self.off_R = 0

    def add_abs(self, a):
        left_max = -self.L[0] + self.off_L if self.L else float('-inf')
        right_min = self.R[0] + self.off_R if self.R else float('inf')

        if a < left_max:
            self.min_f += abs(left_max - a)
            heapq.heappush(self.L, -(a - self.off_L))
            heapq.heappush(self.L, -(a - self.off_L))
            val = -heapq.heappop(self.L) + self.off_L
            heapq.heappush(self.R, val - self.off_R)
        elif a > right_min:
            self.min_f += abs(a - right_min)
            heapq.heappush(self.R, a - self.off_R)
            heapq.heappush(self.R, a - self.off_R)
            val = heapq.heappop(self.R) + self.off_R
            heapq.heappush(self.L, -(val - self.off_L))
        else:
            heapq.heappush(self.L, -(a - self.off_L))
            heapq.heappush(self.R, a - self.off_R)

    def shift_range(self, dl, dr):
        self.off_L += dl
        self.off_R += dr

# Example: Make array strictly increasing (CF 713C)
def solve_713c():
    import sys
    n = int(sys.stdin.readline())
    a = list(map(int, sys.stdin.readline().split()))
    st = SlopeTrick()
    for i in range(n):
        b_i = a[i] - i
        st.add_abs(b_i)
        st.R = [] # Prefix minimum: clear positive slopes
    print(st.min_f)
```

<a id="topic-23"></a>
### 23. Decoupled Sweeps & Chebyshev-Manhattan Equivalence

> **Section:** §20 Greedy Intermediate (Slope Trick, WQS)  
> **Phase:** Phase 5 — Dynamic Programming & Advanced Greedy  
> **Contest ROI:** A-tier | **Rating Range:** 1900–2300 | **Frequency:** Common in Div.1 A/B and Div.2 D/E  
> **Prerequisites:** Coordinate systems, prefix sums, sorting  

---

#### 1. Contest Payoff & Why It Matters

- Contest ROI: A-tier
- Frequency: Common in Div.1 A/B and Div.2 D/E
- Typical CF/ICPC rating range: 1900–2300
- Prerequisites: Coordinate systems, prefix sums, sorting

---

#### 2. Theoretical Deep-Dive & Mathematical Mechanics

##### Overview

Intermediate Greedy techniques bridge convex optimization and dynamic programming: Decoupled Dimension Sweeps, Slope Trick (piecewise linear convex function maintenance via priority queues), and WQS Binary Search (Alien's Trick).

##### Fundamental Theorems & Invariants

- **Additive Independence & Chebyshev Transform:** In $\ell_1$ (Manhattan) and $\ell_\infty$ (Chebyshev) metrics, coordinate transformations $(x', y') = (x+y, x-y)$ decouple multi-dimensional distance minimization into independent 1D median problems.
- **Slope Trick Invariant:** Any continuous piecewise linear convex function $f(x)$ with integer slopes can be represented by its minimum value and two priority queues storing the inflection points where the slope changes by $+1$ and $-1$. Adding $|x - a|$ updates slope inflection points in $\mathcal{O}(\log N)$.
- **Alien's Trick / WQS Binary Search:** If the optimal value $OPT(k)$ as a function of choice count $k$ is strictly concave/convex, introducing a Lagrange multiplier penalty $\lambda$ removes the exact-count constraint, binary searching $\lambda$ in $\mathcal{O}(\text{BaseDP} \cdot \log(\text{Range})))$.

##### Algorithmic Execution Blueprint

1. **Chebyshev Transformation:** Map each point $(x, y) \to (x+y, x-y)$. The maximum Chebyshev distance $\max(|x_1-x_2|, |y_1-y_2|)$ becomes $\frac{1}{2}(|x'_1-x'_2| + |y'_1-y'_2|)$.
2. **Slope Trick Maintenance:** Maintain left heap $L$ (max-heap) and right heap $R$ (min-heap). To add $|x - a|$: if $a < L.\text{top}()$, add cost $L.\text{top}() - a$, push $a$ twice to $L$, and move $L.\text{top}()$ to $R$.
3. **WQS Binary Search:** Binary search penalty $\lambda$. Run unconstrained DP where each selection costs $+\lambda$. If optimal selections count $> k$, increase $\lambda$.

##### Closed-Form Invariant Equations

$$f_{new}(x) = f(x) + |x - a|, \quad \text{WQS}: \min_k \big(dp[k] - \lambda k\big) \iff \text{Lagrangian Duality}$$

##### Structural Edge Cases & Assumptions

- Slope Trick requires the underlying objective function to be strictly convex; non-convex cost functions require general CHT or DP.
- In WQS Binary Search, collinear points (flat segments in the convex hull of $OPT(k)$) require careful tie-breaking (preferring max count or min count) to avoid binary search oscillation.

---

#### 3. Core Intuition & Problem Framing

In $D$-dimensional optimization, problems often appear coupled because the objective function involves multiple variables simultaneously. However, if the objective function $F(x, y)$ can be algebraically partitioned into additive independent components:
$$F(x, y) = f(x) + g(y)$$
and the search space constraint domain is a Cartesian product $S = X \times Y$ (i.e., choosing $x$ does not restrict the valid choices for $y$), then:
$$\min_{(x, y) \in S} F(x, y) = \min_{x \in X} f(x) + \min_{y \in Y} g(y)$$
This reduces a $D$-dimensional search of size $O(N^D)$ into $D$ independent 1-dimensional searches of size $O(N)$.

---

#### 4. Proof Sketches & Recurrences

```python
def solve_chebyshev_center(points):
    """
    Finds point (X, Y) minimizing sum of Chebyshev distances to all points.
    points: list of (x, y)
    """
    # Transform to Manhattan plane
    xs_prime = sorted([x + y for x, y in points])
    ys_prime = sorted([x - y for x, y in points])
    n = len(points)
    
    # In Manhattan plane, optimal point is the median of each axis
    med_x_prime = xs_prime[n // 2]
    med_y_prime = ys_prime[n // 2]
    
    # Transform back: x = (x' + y') / 2, y = (x' - y') / 2
    best_x = (med_x_prime + med_y_prime) / 2.0
    best_y = (med_x_prime - med_y_prime) / 2.0
    return best_x, best_y
```

```cpp
// C++17 — 2D Decoupled Manhattan Center
#include <bits/stdc++.h>
using namespace std;

long long min_manhattan_distance_sum(vector<pair<long long, long long>>& pts) {
    int n = pts.size();
    vector<long long> xs(n), ys(n);
    for (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
        xs[i] = pts[i].first;
        ys[i] = pts[i].second;
    }
    sort(xs.begin(), xs.end());
    sort(ys.begin(), ys.end());
    
    long long med_x = xs[n / 2];
    long long med_y = ys[n / 2];
    
    long long total_dist = 0;
    for (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
        total_dist += abs(xs[i] - med_x) + abs(ys[i] - med_y);
    }
    return total_dist;
}
```

---

## 2. 1D, 2D & Weighted Median Invariants

---

#### 5. Contest-Ready Implementation Templates

##### C++17 Template

```cpp
// C++17 — 2D Decoupled Manhattan Center
#include <bits/stdc++.h>
using namespace std;

long long min_manhattan_distance_sum(vector<pair<long long, long long>>& pts) {
    int n = pts.size();
    vector<long long> xs(n), ys(n);
    for (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
        xs[i] = pts[i].first;
        ys[i] = pts[i].second;
    }
    sort(xs.begin(), xs.end());
    sort(ys.begin(), ys.end());
    
    long long med_x = xs[n / 2];
    long long med_y = ys[n / 2];
    
    long long total_dist = 0;
    for (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
        total_dist += abs(xs[i] - med_x) + abs(ys[i] - med_y);
    }
    return total_dist;
}
```

##### Python 3 Template

```python
def solve_chebyshev_center(points):
    """
    Finds point (X, Y) minimizing sum of Chebyshev distances to all points.
    points: list of (x, y)
    """
    # Transform to Manhattan plane
    xs_prime = sorted([x + y for x, y in points])
    ys_prime = sorted([x - y for x, y in points])
    n = len(points)
    
    # In Manhattan plane, optimal point is the median of each axis
    med_x_prime = xs_prime[n // 2]
    med_y_prime = ys_prime[n // 2]
    
    # Transform back: x = (x' + y') / 2, y = (x' - y') / 2
    best_x = (med_x_prime + med_y_prime) / 2.0
    best_y = (med_x_prime - med_y_prime) / 2.0
    return best_x, best_y
```

---

#### 6. Recognition Trigger Signals & Heuristics

- When costs involve the maximum metric ($L_\infty$ or Chebyshev distance):
$$D_{\infty}((x_1, y_1), (x_2, y_2)) = \max(|x_1 - x_2|, |y_1 - y_2|)$$
the components are coupled by the $\max$ operator. We decouple them by rotating the coordinate axes by $45^\circ$ and scaling by $\sqrt{2}$:
$$(x', y') = (x + y, x - y)$$
Under this transformation, the Chebyshev distance in $(x, y)$ becomes the Manhattan distance ($L_1$) in $(x', y')$ divided by 2:
$$\max(|x_1 - x_2|, |y_1 - y_2|) = \frac{|(x_1 + y_1) - (x_2 + y_2)| + |(x_1 - y_1) - (x_2 - y_2)|}{2} = \frac{|x'_1 - x'_2| + |y'_1 - y'_2|}{2}$$
Now, $x'$ and $y'$ are completely additive and independent! We can sort $x'$ and $y'$ separately and solve with simple prefix sums or median sweeps.

<a id="topic-24"></a>
### 24. Median as the $L_1$ Norm Minimizer

> **Section:** §20 Greedy Intermediate (Slope Trick, WQS)  
> **Phase:** Phase 5 — Dynamic Programming & Advanced Greedy  
> **Contest ROI:** S-tier | **Rating Range:** 1600–2200 | **Frequency:** High  

---

#### 1. Contest Payoff & Why It Matters

- Contest ROI: S-tier
- Frequency: High
- Rating Range: 1500–2100
- Core Invariant: Balancing subgradient sign counts

---

#### 2. Theoretical Deep-Dive & Mathematical Mechanics

##### Overview

Intermediate Greedy techniques bridge convex optimization and dynamic programming: Decoupled Dimension Sweeps, Slope Trick (piecewise linear convex function maintenance via priority queues), and WQS Binary Search (Alien's Trick).

##### Fundamental Theorems & Invariants

- **Additive Independence & Chebyshev Transform:** In $\ell_1$ (Manhattan) and $\ell_\infty$ (Chebyshev) metrics, coordinate transformations $(x', y') = (x+y, x-y)$ decouple multi-dimensional distance minimization into independent 1D median problems.
- **Slope Trick Invariant:** Any continuous piecewise linear convex function $f(x)$ with integer slopes can be represented by its minimum value and two priority queues storing the inflection points where the slope changes by $+1$ and $-1$. Adding $|x - a|$ updates slope inflection points in $\mathcal{O}(\log N)$.
- **Alien's Trick / WQS Binary Search:** If the optimal value $OPT(k)$ as a function of choice count $k$ is strictly concave/convex, introducing a Lagrange multiplier penalty $\lambda$ removes the exact-count constraint, binary searching $\lambda$ in $\mathcal{O}(\text{BaseDP} \cdot \log(\text{Range})))$.

##### Algorithmic Execution Blueprint

1. **Chebyshev Transformation:** Map each point $(x, y) \to (x+y, x-y)$. The maximum Chebyshev distance $\max(|x_1-x_2|, |y_1-y_2|)$ becomes $\frac{1}{2}(|x'_1-x'_2| + |y'_1-y'_2|)$.
2. **Slope Trick Maintenance:** Maintain left heap $L$ (max-heap) and right heap $R$ (min-heap). To add $|x - a|$: if $a < L.\text{top}()$, add cost $L.\text{top}() - a$, push $a$ twice to $L$, and move $L.\text{top}()$ to $R$.
3. **WQS Binary Search:** Binary search penalty $\lambda$. Run unconstrained DP where each selection costs $+\lambda$. If optimal selections count $> k$, increase $\lambda$.

##### Closed-Form Invariant Equations

$$f_{new}(x) = f(x) + |x - a|, \quad \text{WQS}: \min_k \big(dp[k] - \lambda k\big) \iff \text{Lagrangian Duality}$$

##### Structural Edge Cases & Assumptions

- Slope Trick requires the underlying objective function to be strictly convex; non-convex cost functions require general CHT or DP.
- In WQS Binary Search, collinear points (flat segments in the convex hull of $OPT(k)$) require careful tie-breaking (preferring max count or min count) to avoid binary search oscillation.

---

#### 3. Core Intuition & Problem Framing

Consider the convex cost function $C(x) = \sum_{i=1}^N |x - a_i|$ with sorted $a_1 \le a_2 \le \dots \le a_N$.
Take the derivative (subgradient) with respect to $x$:
$$\frac{d}{dx} |x - a_i| = \text{sgn}(x - a_i) = \begin{cases} +1 & \text{if } x > a_i \\ -1 & \text{if } x < a_i \end{cases}$$
Summing across all points:
$$\frac{d}{dx} C(x) = \sum_{i=1}^N \text{sgn}(x - a_i) = (\# \text{ points to the left of } x) - (\# \text{ points to the right of } x)$$
- If $x$ is to the left of the median, $(\# \text{ left}) < (\# \text{ right}) \implies C'(x) < 0$, so moving $x$ right **decreases** cost.
- If $x$ is to the right of the median, $(\# \text{ left}) > (\# \text{ right}) \implies C'(x) > 0$, so moving $x$ left **decreases** cost.
- The global minimum occurs where $C'(x) = 0 \iff (\# \text{ left}) = (\# \text{ right})$, which is precisely the **Median**.

---

#### 4. Proof Sketches & Recurrences

To maintain the median of a dynamic set under insertions and deletions in $O(\log N)$:
1. Maintain **`L`** (Max-Heap) storing the smaller half and **`R`** (Min-Heap) storing the larger half.
2. Invariant: $\text{size}(L) \in \{\text{size}(R), \text{size}(R) + 1\}$ and $\max(L) \le \min(R)$.
3. Median is always $\text{top}(L)$.

```cpp
// C++17 — Dynamic Running Median with Lazy Deletions
#include <bits/stdc++.h>
using namespace std;

class DynamicMedian {
    priority_queue<long long> L; // max-heap
    priority_queue<long long, vector<long long>, greater<long long>> R; // min-heap
    unordered_map<long long, int> delayed;
    int size_L = 0, size_R = 0;

    void clean(auto& pq, int sign) {
        while (!pq.empty()) {
            long long val = pq.top();
            if (delayed[val] > 0) {
                delayed[val]--;
                pq.pop();
            } else break;
        }
    }

    void rebalance() {
        if (size_L > size_R + 1) {
            R.push(L.top()); L.pop();
            size_L--; size_R++;
            clean(L, 1);
        } else if (size_L < size_R) {
            L.push(R.top()); R.pop();
            size_L++; size_R--;
            clean(R, -1);
        }
    }

public:
    void insert(long long x) {
        if (L.empty() || x <= L.top()) {
            L.push(x); size_L++;
        } else {
            R.push(x); size_R++;
        }
        rebalance();
    }

    void erase(long long x) {
        delayed[x]++;
        if (!L.empty() && x <= L.top()) size_L--;
        else size_R--;
        clean(L, 1); clean(R, -1);
        rebalance();
    }

    long long get_median() {
        clean(L, 1);
        return L.top();
    }
};
```

---

## 3. Prefix-Suffix Sweeps & Block Compression

---

#### 5. Contest-Ready Implementation Templates

##### C++17 Template

```cpp
// C++17 — Dynamic Running Median with Lazy Deletions
#include <bits/stdc++.h>
using namespace std;

class DynamicMedian {
    priority_queue<long long> L; // max-heap
    priority_queue<long long, vector<long long>, greater<long long>> R; // min-heap
    unordered_map<long long, int> delayed;
    int size_L = 0, size_R = 0;

    void clean(auto& pq, int sign) {
        while (!pq.empty()) {
            long long val = pq.top();
            if (delayed[val] > 0) {
                delayed[val]--;
                pq.pop();
            } else break;
        }
    }

    void rebalance() {
        if (size_L > size_R + 1) {
            R.push(L.top()); L.pop();
            size_L--; size_R++;
            clean(L, 1);
        } else if (size_L < size_R) {
            L.push(R.top()); R.pop();
            size_L++; size_R--;
            clean(R, -1);
        }
    }

public:
    void insert(long long x) {
        if (L.empty() || x <= L.top()) {
            L.push(x); size_L++;
        } else {
            R.push(x); size_R++;
        }
        rebalance();
    }

    void erase(long long x) {
        delayed[x]++;
        if (!L.empty() && x <= L.top()) size_L--;
        else size_R--;
        clean(L, 1); clean(R, -1);
        rebalance();
    }

    long long get_median() {
        clean(L, 1);
        return L.top();
    }
};
```

---

#### 6. Recognition Trigger Signals & Heuristics

- If each point $a_i$ carries a positive weight $w_i$, the cost is $C(x) = \sum_{i=1}^N w_i |x - a_i|$.
The derivative becomes:
$$\frac{d}{dx} C(x) = \sum_{a_i < x} w_i - \sum_{a_i > x} w_i$$
The optimum is reached at the **Weighted Median**: the smallest $a_k$ such that the cumulative weight reaches at least half the total weight:
$$\sum_{i=1}^k w_i \ge \frac{1}{2} \sum_{i=1}^N w_i$$

<a id="topic-25"></a>
### 25. Simultaneous Past-Future Queries and State Space Reduction

> **Section:** §20 Greedy Intermediate (Slope Trick, WQS)  
> **Phase:** Phase 5 — Dynamic Programming & Advanced Greedy  
> **Contest ROI:** B-tier | **Rating Range:** 1600–2200 | **Frequency:** Common  

---

#### 1. Contest Payoff & Why It Matters

- Turns $O(N^2)$ candidate evaluations into $O(N)$ single-pass solutions.
- Precalculates transition states for fast local modifications.

---

#### 2. Theoretical Deep-Dive & Mathematical Mechanics

##### Overview

Intermediate Greedy techniques bridge convex optimization and dynamic programming: Decoupled Dimension Sweeps, Slope Trick (piecewise linear convex function maintenance via priority queues), and WQS Binary Search (Alien's Trick).

##### Fundamental Theorems & Invariants

- **Additive Independence & Chebyshev Transform:** In $\ell_1$ (Manhattan) and $\ell_\infty$ (Chebyshev) metrics, coordinate transformations $(x', y') = (x+y, x-y)$ decouple multi-dimensional distance minimization into independent 1D median problems.
- **Slope Trick Invariant:** Any continuous piecewise linear convex function $f(x)$ with integer slopes can be represented by its minimum value and two priority queues storing the inflection points where the slope changes by $+1$ and $-1$. Adding $|x - a|$ updates slope inflection points in $\mathcal{O}(\log N)$.
- **Alien's Trick / WQS Binary Search:** If the optimal value $OPT(k)$ as a function of choice count $k$ is strictly concave/convex, introducing a Lagrange multiplier penalty $\lambda$ removes the exact-count constraint, binary searching $\lambda$ in $\mathcal{O}(\text{BaseDP} \cdot \log(\text{Range})))$.

##### Algorithmic Execution Blueprint

1. **Chebyshev Transformation:** Map each point $(x, y) \to (x+y, x-y)$. The maximum Chebyshev distance $\max(|x_1-x_2|, |y_1-y_2|)$ becomes $\frac{1}{2}(|x'_1-x'_2| + |y'_1-y'_2|)$.
2. **Slope Trick Maintenance:** Maintain left heap $L$ (max-heap) and right heap $R$ (min-heap). To add $|x - a|$: if $a < L.\text{top}()$, add cost $L.\text{top}() - a$, push $a$ twice to $L$, and move $L.\text{top}()$ to $R$.
3. **WQS Binary Search:** Binary search penalty $\lambda$. Run unconstrained DP where each selection costs $+\lambda$. If optimal selections count $> k$, increase $\lambda$.

##### Closed-Form Invariant Equations

$$f_{new}(x) = f(x) + |x - a|, \quad \text{WQS}: \min_k \big(dp[k] - \lambda k\big) \iff \text{Lagrangian Duality}$$

##### Structural Edge Cases & Assumptions

- Slope Trick requires the underlying objective function to be strictly convex; non-convex cost functions require general CHT or DP.
- In WQS Binary Search, collinear points (flat segments in the convex hull of $OPT(k)$) require careful tie-breaking (preferring max count or min count) to avoid binary search oscillation.

---

#### 3. Core Intuition & Problem Framing

When evaluating the consequence of removing, replacing, or splitting at index $i$:
1. Compute prefix aggregations $P[i] = P[i-1] \otimes A[i]$.
2. Compute suffix aggregations $S[i] = A[i] \otimes S[i+1]$.
3. The state of the universe without element $i$ is evaluated in $O(1)$ by querying $P[i-1] \otimes S[i+1]$.

---

#### 4. Proof Sketches & Recurrences

- **CSES Stick Lengths** (Task 1074) — Pure 1D median
- **CSES Sliding Window Median** (Task 1076) — Dynamic two-heap median
- **Codeforces 1492C - Maximum Width** — Prefix-suffix greedy matching
- **Codeforces 1630C - Paint the Middle** — Decoupled sweep line on intervals

---

#### 6. Recognition Trigger Signals & Heuristics

- In constructive and greedy string/array problems, operations often affect entire contiguous segments of identical elements. 
Transforming the sequence $A$ into an array of tuples $(value_j, length_j)$ reduces the effective size from $N$ to the number of transitions $M \le N$. The greedy algorithm then sweeps over transitions rather than individual indices.

<a id="topic-29"></a>
### 29. Classical Matroid Archetypes in CP

> **Section:** §20 Greedy Intermediate (Slope Trick, WQS)  
> **Phase:** Phase 5 — Dynamic Programming & Advanced Greedy  
> **Contest ROI:** B-tier | **Rating Range:** 1600–2200 | **Frequency:** Common  

---

#### 1. Contest Payoff & Why It Matters

* $E$: Any set of $n$ elements.
* $\mathcal{I} = \{S \subseteq E \mid |S| \le k\}$.
* *Problem Context:* "Pick at most $k$ elements to maximize sum."

---

#### 2. Theoretical Deep-Dive & Mathematical Mechanics

##### Overview

Intermediate Greedy techniques bridge convex optimization and dynamic programming: Decoupled Dimension Sweeps, Slope Trick (piecewise linear convex function maintenance via priority queues), and WQS Binary Search (Alien's Trick).

##### Fundamental Theorems & Invariants

- **Additive Independence & Chebyshev Transform:** In $\ell_1$ (Manhattan) and $\ell_\infty$ (Chebyshev) metrics, coordinate transformations $(x', y') = (x+y, x-y)$ decouple multi-dimensional distance minimization into independent 1D median problems.
- **Slope Trick Invariant:** Any continuous piecewise linear convex function $f(x)$ with integer slopes can be represented by its minimum value and two priority queues storing the inflection points where the slope changes by $+1$ and $-1$. Adding $|x - a|$ updates slope inflection points in $\mathcal{O}(\log N)$.
- **Alien's Trick / WQS Binary Search:** If the optimal value $OPT(k)$ as a function of choice count $k$ is strictly concave/convex, introducing a Lagrange multiplier penalty $\lambda$ removes the exact-count constraint, binary searching $\lambda$ in $\mathcal{O}(\text{BaseDP} \cdot \log(\text{Range})))$.

##### Algorithmic Execution Blueprint

1. **Chebyshev Transformation:** Map each point $(x, y) \to (x+y, x-y)$. The maximum Chebyshev distance $\max(|x_1-x_2|, |y_1-y_2|)$ becomes $\frac{1}{2}(|x'_1-x'_2| + |y'_1-y'_2|)$.
2. **Slope Trick Maintenance:** Maintain left heap $L$ (max-heap) and right heap $R$ (min-heap). To add $|x - a|$: if $a < L.\text{top}()$, add cost $L.\text{top}() - a$, push $a$ twice to $L$, and move $L.\text{top}()$ to $R$.
3. **WQS Binary Search:** Binary search penalty $\lambda$. Run unconstrained DP where each selection costs $+\lambda$. If optimal selections count $> k$, increase $\lambda$.

##### Closed-Form Invariant Equations

$$f_{new}(x) = f(x) + |x - a|, \quad \text{WQS}: \min_k \big(dp[k] - \lambda k\big) \iff \text{Lagrangian Duality}$$

##### Structural Edge Cases & Assumptions

- Slope Trick requires the underlying objective function to be strictly convex; non-convex cost functions require general CHT or DP.
- In WQS Binary Search, collinear points (flat segments in the convex hull of $OPT(k)$) require careful tie-breaking (preferring max count or min count) to avoid binary search oscillation.

---

#### 3. Core Intuition & Problem Framing

* $E$: Edges of an undirected graph $G = (V, E)$.
* $\mathcal{I}$: Subsets of edges that contain **no cycles** (forests).
* *Bases:* Spanning forests (or Spanning Trees if $G$ is connected).
* *Circuits:* Simple cycles.
* *Greedy Algorithm:* **Kruskal's Algorithm**. Kruskal is literally the Rado-Edmonds greedy algorithm on a Graphic Matroid.

---

#### 4. Proof Sketches & Recurrences

* $E$ is partitioned into disjoint categories: $E = B_1 \sqcup B_2 \sqcup \dots \sqcup B_k$.
* Bounds $d_1, d_2, \dots, d_k$ for each category.
* $\mathcal{I} = \{S \subseteq E \mid |S \cap B_i| \le d_i \text{ for all } i\}$.
* *Problem Context:* "Select items of various categories, taking at most $d_i$ items of category $i$."

---

---

#### 6. Recognition Trigger Signals & Heuristics

- $E$: A set of vectors over a field $\mathbb{F}$ (e.g., $\mathbb{F}_2$ for XOR).
- $\mathcal{I}$: Subsets of vectors that are **linearly independent**.
- Circuits:* Minimally linearly dependent sets of vectors.
- Greedy Algorithm:* **XOR Linear Basis** (Gaussian elimination over $\mathbb{F}_2$).

---

### 30. Python Implementation: The Generic Matroid Greedy

> **Section:** §20 Greedy Intermediate (Slope Trick, WQS)

> **Contest ROI:** B-tier | **Rating Range:** 1600–2200 | **Frequency:** Common  

---

#### 2. Theoretical Deep-Dive & Mathematical Mechanics

##### Overview

Intermediate Greedy techniques bridge convex optimization and dynamic programming: Decoupled Dimension Sweeps, Slope Trick (piecewise linear convex function maintenance via priority queues), and WQS Binary Search (Alien's Trick).

- **Slope Trick Invariant:** Any continuous piecewise linear convex function $f(x)$ with integer slopes can be represented by its minimum value and two priority queues storing the inflection points where the slope changes by $+1$ and $-1$. Adding $|x - a|$ updates slope inflection points in $\mathcal{O}(\log N)$.
- **Alien's Trick / WQS Binary Search:** If the optimal value $OPT(k)$ as a function of choice count $k$ is strictly concave/convex, introducing a Lagrange multiplier penalty $\lambda$ removes the exact-count constraint, binary searching $\lambda$ in $\mathcal{O}(\text{BaseDP} \cdot \log(\text{Range})))$.

##### Algorithmic Execution Blueprint

1. **Chebyshev Transformation:** Map each point $(x, y) \to (x+y, x-y)$. The maximum Chebyshev distance $\max(|x_1-x_2|, |y_1-y_2|)$ becomes $\frac{1}{2}(|x'_1-x'_2| + |y'_1-y'_2|)$.
2. **Slope Trick Maintenance:** Maintain left heap $L$ (max-heap) and right heap $R$ (min-heap). To add $|x - a|$: if $a < L.\text{top}()$, add cost $L.\text{top}() - a$, push $a$ twice to $L$, and move $L.\text{top}()$ to $R$.
3. **WQS Binary Search:** Binary search penalty $\lambda$. Run unconstrained DP where each selection costs $+\lambda$. If optimal selections count $> k$, increase $\lambda$.

##### Closed-Form Invariant Equations

$$f_{new}(x) = f(x) + |x - a|, \quad \text{WQS}: \min_k \big(dp[k] - \lambda k\big) \iff \text{Lagrangian Duality}$$

---

#### 5. Contest-Ready Implementation Templates

##### Python 3 Template

```python
import sys

def matroid_greedy(elements, weights, oracle_can_add):
    """
    elements: list of elements in ground set E
    weights: list of weights corresponding to elements
    oracle_can_add: function(current_set, candidate_element) -> bool
                    returns True if current_set + {candidate} in I
    """
    # 1. Sort indices in descending order of weight
    indices = list(range(len(elements)))
    indices.sort(key=lambda i: weights[i], reverse=True)
    
    independent_set = []
    total_weight = 0
    
    # 2. Greedily expand the independent set
    for idx in indices:
        elem = elements[idx]
        if oracle_can_add(independent_set, elem):
            independent_set.append(elem)
            total_weight += weights[idx]
            
    return independent_set, total_weight
```

##### Algorithmic Execution Blueprint

1. **Chebyshev Transformation:** Map each point $(x, y) \to (x+y, x-y)$. The maximum Chebyshev distance $\max(|x_1-x_2|, |y_1-y_2|)$ becomes $\frac{1}{2}(|x'_1-x'_2| + |y'_1-y'_2|)$.
2. **Slope Trick Maintenance:** Maintain left heap $L$ (max-heap) and right heap $R$ (min-heap). To add $|x - a|$: if $a < L.\text{top}()$, add cost $L.\text{top}() - a$, push $a$ twice to $L$, and move $L.\text{top}()$ to $R$.
3. **WQS Binary Search:** Binary search penalty $\lambda$. Run unconstrained DP where each selection costs $+\lambda$. If optimal selections count $> k$, increase $\lambda$.

##### Closed-Form Invariant Equations

$$f_{new}(x) = f(x) + |x - a|, \quad \text{WQS}: \min_k \big(dp[k] - \lambda k\big) \iff \text{Lagrangian Duality}$$

##### Structural Edge Cases & Assumptions

- Slope Trick requires the underlying objective function to be strictly convex; non-convex cost functions require general CHT or DP.
- In WQS Binary Search, collinear points (flat segments in the convex hull of $OPT(k)$) require careful tie-breaking (preferring max count or min count) to avoid binary search oscillation.

---

#### 5. Contest-Ready Implementation Templates

##### Python 3 Template

```python
class GraphicMatroidRank:
    def __init__(self, n):
        self.n = n

    def evaluate_rank(self, edges, subset_indices):
        """
        Calculates the rank of a specific subset of edges.
        Rank = number of vertices (n) - number of connected components.
        """
        parent = list(range(self.n + 1))
        
        def find(i):
            if parent[i] == i: return i
            parent[i] = find(parent[i])
            return parent[i]
            
        def union(i, j):
            root_i = find(i)
            root_j = find(j)
            if root_i != root_j:
                parent[root_i] = root_j
                return True
            return False

        rank = 0
        for idx in subset_indices:
            u, v = edges[idx]
            # If union is successful, the edge is independent of the current forest
            if union(u, v):
                rank += 1
                
        return rank
```

### 36. Identifying Submodular / Dual Problems

#### 2. Theoretical Deep-Dive & Mathematical Mechanics

##### Overview

Intermediate Greedy techniques bridge convex optimization and dynamic programming: Decoupled Dimension Sweeps, Slope Trick (piecewise linear convex function maintenance via priority queues), and WQS Binary Search (Alien's Trick).

##### Fundamental Theorems & Invariants

- **Additive Independence & Chebyshev Transform:** In $\ell_1$ (Manhattan) and $\ell_\infty$ (Chebyshev) metrics, coordinate transformations $(x', y') = (x+y, x-y)$ decouple multi-dimensional distance minimization into independent 1D median problems.
- **Slope Trick Invariant:** Any continuous piecewise linear convex function $f(x)$ with integer slopes can be represented by its minimum value and two priority queues storing the inflection points where the slope changes by $+1$ and $-1$. Adding $|x - a|$ updates slope inflection points in $\mathcal{O}(\log N)$.
- **Alien's Trick / WQS Binary Search:** If the optimal value $OPT(k)$ as a function of choice count $k$ is strictly concave/convex, introducing a Lagrange multiplier penalty $\lambda$ removes the exact-count constraint, binary searching $\lambda$ in $\mathcal{O}(\text{BaseDP} \cdot \log(\text{Range})))$.

##### Algorithmic Execution Blueprint

1. **Chebyshev Transformation:** Map each point $(x, y) \to (x+y, x-y)$. The maximum Chebyshev distance $\max(|x_1-x_2|, |y_1-y_2|)$ becomes $\frac{1}{2}(|x'_1-x'_2| + |y'_1-y'_2|)$.
2. **Slope Trick Maintenance:** Maintain left heap $L$ (max-heap) and right heap $R$ (min-heap). To add $|x - a|$: if $a < L.\text{top}()$, add cost $L.\text{top}() - a$, push $a$ twice to $L$, and move $L.\text{top}()$ to $R$.
3. **WQS Binary Search:** Binary search penalty $\lambda$. Run unconstrained DP where each selection costs $+\lambda$. If optimal selections count $> k$, increase $\lambda$.

##### Closed-Form Invariant Equations

$$f_{new}(x) = f(x) + |x - a|, \quad \text{WQS}: \min_k \big(dp[k] - \lambda k\big) \iff \text{Lagrangian Duality}$$

##### Structural Edge Cases & Assumptions

- Slope Trick requires the underlying objective function to be strictly convex; non-convex cost functions require general CHT or DP.
- In WQS Binary Search, collinear points (flat segments in the convex hull of $OPT(k)$) require careful tie-breaking (preferring max count or min count) to avoid binary search oscillation.

##### Overview

##### Fundamental Theorems & Invariants

##### Algorithmic Execution Blueprint

1. **Chebyshev Transformation:** Map each point $(x, y) \to (x+y, x-y)$. The maximum Chebyshev distance $\max(|x_1-x_2|, |y_1-y_2|)$ becomes $\frac{1}{2}(|x'_1-x'_2| + |y'_1-y'_2|)$.
2. **Slope Trick Maintenance:** Maintain left heap $L$ (max-heap) and right heap $R$ (min-heap). To add $|x - a|$: if $a < L.\text{top}()$, add cost $L.\text{top}() - a$, push $a$ twice to $L$, and move $L.\text{top}()$ to $R$.
3. **WQS Binary Search:** Binary search penalty $\lambda$. Run unconstrained DP where each selection costs $+\lambda$. If optimal selections count $> k$, increase $\lambda$.

---

#### 5. Contest-Ready Implementation Templates

##### Python 3 Template

```python
from collections import deque

def matroid_intersection(elements, oracle1, oracle2):
    I = set()
    
    while True:
        X1 = []
        X2 = []
        adj = {e: [] for e in elements}
        
        # 1. Build the Exchange Graph
        for y in elements:
            if y in I: continue
            
            # Check Sources (X1) and Sinks (X2)
            if oracle1.can_add(I, y): X1.append(y)
            if oracle2.can_add(I, y): X2.append(y)
            
            # Check Swap Edges
            for x in I:
                I_minus_x = I - {x}
                # M1 Edge: y -> x
                if oracle1.can_add(I_minus_x, y):
                    adj[y].append(x)
                # M2 Edge: x -> y
                if oracle2.can_add(I_minus_x, y):
                    adj[x].append(y)
                    
        # 2. BFS to find shortest path from any X1 to any X2
        queue = deque(X1)
        parent = {start: None for start in X1}
        target = None
        
        while queue and target is None:
            curr = queue.popleft()
            if curr in X2:
                target = curr
                break
            for neighbor in adj[curr]:
                if neighbor not in parent:
                    parent[neighbor] = curr
                    queue.append(neighbor)
                    
        # 3. Augment if a path was found, otherwise terminate
        if target is None:
            break
            
        path = set()
        curr = target
        while curr is not None:
            path.add(curr)
            curr = parent[curr]
            
        # Symmetric difference
        I = I.symmetric_difference(path)
        
    return I
```

3. **WQS Binary Search:** Binary search penalty $\lambda$. Run unconstrained DP where each selection costs $+\lambda$. If optimal selections count $> k$, increase $\lambda$.

##### Closed-Form Invariant Equations

$$f_{new}(x) = f(x) + |x - a|, \quad \text{WQS}: \min_k \big(dp[k] - \lambda k\big) \iff \text{Lagrangian Duality}$$

##### Structural Edge Cases & Assumptions

- Slope Trick requires the underlying objective function to be strictly convex; non-convex cost functions require general CHT or DP.
- In WQS Binary Search, collinear points (flat segments in the convex hull of $OPT(k)$) require careful tie-breaking (preferring max count or min count) to avoid binary search oscillation.

<a id="topic-42"></a>
### 42. What is an Antimatroid?

> **Section:** §20 Greedy Intermediate (Slope Trick, WQS)  
> **Phase:** Phase 5 — Dynamic Programming & Advanced Greedy  
> **Contest ROI:** B-tier | **Rating Range:** 1600–2200 | **Frequency:** Common  

---

#### 2. Theoretical Deep-Dive & Mathematical Mechanics

##### Overview

Intermediate Greedy techniques bridge convex optimization and dynamic programming: Decoupled Dimension Sweeps, Slope Trick (piecewise linear convex function maintenance via priority queues), and WQS Binary Search (Alien's Trick).

##### Fundamental Theorems & Invariants

- **Additive Independence & Chebyshev Transform:** In $\ell_1$ (Manhattan) and $\ell_\infty$ (Chebyshev) metrics, coordinate transformations $(x', y') = (x+y, x-y)$ decouple multi-dimensional distance minimization into independent 1D median problems.
- **Slope Trick Invariant:** Any continuous piecewise linear convex function $f(x)$ with integer slopes can be represented by its minimum value and two priority queues storing the inflection points where the slope changes by $+1$ and $-1$. Adding $|x - a|$ updates slope inflection points in $\mathcal{O}(\log N)$.
- **Alien's Trick / WQS Binary Search:** If the optimal value $OPT(k)$ as a function of choice count $k$ is strictly concave/convex, introducing a Lagrange multiplier penalty $\lambda$ removes the exact-count constraint, binary searching $\lambda$ in $\mathcal{O}(\text{BaseDP} \cdot \log(\text{Range})))$.

##### Algorithmic Execution Blueprint

1. **Chebyshev Transformation:** Map each point $(x, y) \to (x+y, x-y)$. The maximum Chebyshev distance $\max(|x_1-x_2|, |y_1-y_2|)$ becomes $\frac{1}{2}(|x'_1-x'_2| + |y'_1-y'_2|)$.
2. **Slope Trick Maintenance:** Maintain left heap $L$ (max-heap) and right heap $R$ (min-heap). To add $|x - a|$: if $a < L.\text{top}()$, add cost $L.\text{top}() - a$, push $a$ twice to $L$, and move $L.\text{top}()$ to $R$.
3. **WQS Binary Search:** Binary search penalty $\lambda$. Run unconstrained DP where each selection costs $+\lambda$. If optimal selections count $> k$, increase $\lambda$.

##### Closed-Form Invariant Equations

$$f_{new}(x) = f(x) + |x - a|, \quad \text{WQS}: \min_k \big(dp[k] - \lambda k\big) \iff \text{Lagrangian Duality}$$

##### Structural Edge Cases & Assumptions

- Slope Trick requires the underlying objective function to be strictly convex; non-convex cost functions require general CHT or DP.
- In WQS Binary Search, collinear points (flat segments in the convex hull of $OPT(k)$) require careful tie-breaking (preferring max count or min count) to avoid binary search oscillation.

### 45. Python Implementation: Kahn's Algorithm + Priority Queue

> **Section:** §20 Greedy Intermediate (Slope Trick, WQS)  
> **Phase:** Phase 5 — Dynamic Programming & Advanced Greedy  
> **Contest ROI:** B-tier | **Rating Range:** 1600–2200 | **Frequency:** Common  

---

#### 2. Theoretical Deep-Dive & Mathematical Mechanics

##### Overview

Intermediate Greedy techniques bridge convex optimization and dynamic programming: Decoupled Dimension Sweeps, Slope Trick (piecewise linear convex function maintenance via priority queues), and WQS Binary Search (Alien's Trick).

##### Fundamental Theorems & Invariants

- **Additive Independence & Chebyshev Transform:** In $\ell_1$ (Manhattan) and $\ell_\infty$ (Chebyshev) metrics, coordinate transformations $(x', y') = (x+y, x-y)$ decouple multi-dimensional distance minimization into independent 1D median problems.
- **Slope Trick Invariant:** Any continuous piecewise linear convex function $f(x)$ with integer slopes can be represented by its minimum value and two priority queues storing the inflection points where the slope changes by $+1$ and $-1$. Adding $|x - a|$ updates slope inflection points in $\mathcal{O}(\log N)$.
- **Alien's Trick / WQS Binary Search:** If the optimal value $OPT(k)$ as a function of choice count $k$ is strictly concave/convex, introducing a Lagrange multiplier penalty $\lambda$ removes the exact-count constraint, binary searching $\lambda$ in $\mathcal{O}(\text{BaseDP} \cdot \log(\text{Range})))$.

##### Algorithmic Execution Blueprint

1. **Chebyshev Transformation:** Map each point $(x, y) \to (x+y, x-y)$. The maximum Chebyshev distance $\max(|x_1-x_2|, |y_1-y_2|)$ becomes $\frac{1}{2}(|x'_1-x'_2| + |y'_1-y'_2|)$.
2. **Slope Trick Maintenance:** Maintain left heap $L$ (max-heap) and right heap $R$ (min-heap). To add $|x - a|$: if $a < L.\text{top}()$, add cost $L.\text{top}() - a$, push $a$ twice to $L$, and move $L.\text{top}()$ to $R$.
3. **WQS Binary Search:** Binary search penalty $\lambda$. Run unconstrained DP where each selection costs $+\lambda$. If optimal selections count $> k$, increase $\lambda$.

##### Closed-Form Invariant Equations

$$f_{new}(x) = f(x) + |x - a|, \quad \text{WQS}: \min_k \big(dp[k] - \lambda k\big) \iff \text{Lagrangian Duality}$$

##### Structural Edge Cases & Assumptions

- Slope Trick requires the underlying objective function to be strictly convex; non-convex cost functions require general CHT or DP.
- In WQS Binary Search, collinear points (flat segments in the convex hull of $OPT(k)$) require careful tie-breaking (preferring max count or min count) to avoid binary search oscillation.

---

#### 5. Contest-Ready Implementation Templates

##### Python 3 Template

```python
import heapq

def lexicographical_topsort(n, edges):
    # n jobs: 0 to n-1
    adj = {i: [] for i in range(n)}
    in_degree = [0] * n
    
    for u, v in edges:
        adj[u].append(v)
        in_degree[v] += 1
        
    # The Antimatroid "frontier"
    # Use a Min-Heap to greedily pick the "smallest" available job
    pq = []
    for i in range(n):
        if in_degree[i] == 0:
            heapq.heappush(pq, i)
            
    topo_order = []
    
    while pq:
        # Greedy choice: pick the lexicographically smallest valid node
        u = heapq.heappop(pq)
        topo_order.append(u)
        
        for v in adj[u]:
            in_degree[v] -= 1
            if in_degree[v] == 0:
                # v becomes accessible
                heapq.heappush(pq, v)
                
    if len(topo_order) == n:
        return topo_order
    else:
        return [] # Cycle detected, no valid antimatroid sequence
```

<a id="topic-47"></a>
### 47. The Mathematical Origin of WQS Binary Search

> **Section:** §20 Greedy Intermediate (Slope Trick, WQS)  
> **Phase:** Phase 5 — Dynamic Programming & Advanced Greedy  
> **Contest ROI:** S-tier in Grandmaster/IOI contests | **Rating Range:** 1600–2200 | **Frequency:** Essential for high-tier CF (2200–2600+)  
> **Prerequisites:** Convexity/concavity, binary search on real/integer slopes, standard DP  

---

#### 1. Contest Payoff & Why It Matters

- Contest ROI: S-tier in Grandmaster/IOI contests
- Frequency: Essential for high-tier CF (2200–2600+)
- Core Superpower: **Removes an entire dimension** from DP state (e.g., $O(N K)$ becomes $O(N \log V)$)
- Prerequisites: Convexity/concavity, binary search on real/integer slopes, standard DP

---

#### 2. Theoretical Deep-Dive & Mathematical Mechanics

##### Overview

Intermediate Greedy techniques bridge convex optimization and dynamic programming: Decoupled Dimension Sweeps, Slope Trick (piecewise linear convex function maintenance via priority queues), and WQS Binary Search (Alien's Trick).

##### Fundamental Theorems & Invariants

- **Additive Independence & Chebyshev Transform:** In $\ell_1$ (Manhattan) and $\ell_\infty$ (Chebyshev) metrics, coordinate transformations $(x', y') = (x+y, x-y)$ decouple multi-dimensional distance minimization into independent 1D median problems.
- **Slope Trick Invariant:** Any continuous piecewise linear convex function $f(x)$ with integer slopes can be represented by its minimum value and two priority queues storing the inflection points where the slope changes by $+1$ and $-1$. Adding $|x - a|$ updates slope inflection points in $\mathcal{O}(\log N)$.
- **Alien's Trick / WQS Binary Search:** If the optimal value $OPT(k)$ as a function of choice count $k$ is strictly concave/convex, introducing a Lagrange multiplier penalty $\lambda$ removes the exact-count constraint, binary searching $\lambda$ in $\mathcal{O}(\text{BaseDP} \cdot \log(\text{Range})))$.

##### Algorithmic Execution Blueprint

1. **Chebyshev Transformation:** Map each point $(x, y) \to (x+y, x-y)$. The maximum Chebyshev distance $\max(|x_1-x_2|, |y_1-y_2|)$ becomes $\frac{1}{2}(|x'_1-x'_2| + |y'_1-y'_2|)$.
2. **Slope Trick Maintenance:** Maintain left heap $L$ (max-heap) and right heap $R$ (min-heap). To add $|x - a|$: if $a < L.\text{top}()$, add cost $L.\text{top}() - a$, push $a$ twice to $L$, and move $L.\text{top}()$ to $R$.
3. **WQS Binary Search:** Binary search penalty $\lambda$. Run unconstrained DP where each selection costs $+\lambda$. If optimal selections count $> k$, increase $\lambda$.

##### Closed-Form Invariant Equations

$$f_{new}(x) = f(x) + |x - a|, \quad \text{WQS}: \min_k \big(dp[k] - \lambda k\big) \iff \text{Lagrangian Duality}$$

##### Structural Edge Cases & Assumptions

- Slope Trick requires the underlying objective function to be strictly convex; non-convex cost functions require general CHT or DP.
- In WQS Binary Search, collinear points (flat segments in the convex hull of $OPT(k)$) require careful tie-breaking (preferring max count or min count) to avoid binary search oscillation.

---

#### 3. Core Intuition & Problem Framing

Suppose we wish to solve:
$$\min_{x \in \mathcal{X}, \, \text{count}(x) = K} \text{cost}(x)$$
Let $F(k)$ be the minimum cost achieved using **exactly $k$ items**. 
Computing $F(K)$ directly typically requires storing the count $k \in [0, K]$ in the DP state, yielding $O(N K)$ time. When $N, K \le 10^5$, this TLEs.

---

#### 4. Proof Sketches & Recurrences

Plot the points $(k, F(k))$ in the Cartesian plane:
1. The line $y = -\lambda k + c$ has slope $-\lambda$ and $y$-intercept $c$.
2. Finding $\min_k (F(k) + \lambda k)$ is equivalent to finding the line of slope $-\lambda$ that touches the lower boundary of the point set from below.
3. The point of tangency $(k^*, F(k^*))$ gives:
   - $k^* = \arg\min_k (F(k) + \lambda k)$
   - $c^* = g(\lambda) = F(k^*) + \lambda k^*$
4. If $F(k)$ is **strictly convex** (the slopes between consecutive points $F(k+1) - F(k)$ are strictly increasing):
   - Higher $\lambda \implies$ higher penalty per item $\implies$ fewer items chosen ($k^*$ decreases).
   - Lower $\lambda \implies$ lower penalty per item $\implies$ more items chosen ($k^*$ increases).
5. Because $k^*(\lambda)$ is **monotone** with respect to $\lambda$, we can **binary search for the optimal slope $\lambda^*$** that forces $k^*(\lambda^*) = K$!
6. Once $\lambda^*$ is found, the true answer is recovered in $O(1)$:
   $$F(K) = g(\lambda^*) - \lambda^* \cdot K$$

```
   F(k) ^
        |        (1, F(1))
        |          \
        |           \    (2, F(2))  <- Tangent point where slope = -lambda
        |            \___/
        |                \___ (3, F(3))
        |                    \___
        |                        (4, F(4))
        +-----------------------------------> k
```

---

## 2. Dealing with Collinear Points & Tie-Breaking (LIP)

---

#### 6. Recognition Trigger Signals & Heuristics

- Instead of forcing $\text{count}(x) = K$, we introduce a cost **penalty $\lambda$** per item chosen and solve the **unconstrained problem**:
$$g(\lambda) = \min_{x \in \mathcal{X}} \big( \text{cost}(x) + \lambda \cdot \text{count}(x) \big)$$
In terms of the function $F(k)$:
$$g(\lambda) = \min_{k} \big( F(k) + \lambda \cdot k \big)$$

---

<a id="topic-48"></a>
### 48. When Multiple Counts Share the Same Optimal Tangency

> **Section:** §20 Greedy Intermediate (Slope Trick, WQS)  
> **Phase:** Phase 5 — Dynamic Programming & Advanced Greedy  
> **Contest ROI:** B-tier | **Rating Range:** 1600–2200 | **Frequency:** Common  

---

#### 1. Contest Payoff & Why It Matters

If $F(k)$ is not strictly convex (i.e., several points $(k_1, F(k_1)), (k_2, F(k_2)), \dots$ are collinear with slope $-\lambda$), then for that exact $\lambda$:
$$F(k_1) + \lambda k_1 = F(k_2) + \lambda k_2 = \dots = g(\lambda)$$
The binary search may jump directly from $k < K$ to $k > K$ without ever hitting $k = K$ exactly.

---

#### 2. Theoretical Deep-Dive & Mathematical Mechanics

##### Overview

Intermediate Greedy techniques bridge convex optimization and dynamic programming: Decoupled Dimension Sweeps, Slope Trick (piecewise linear convex function maintenance via priority queues), and WQS Binary Search (Alien's Trick).

##### Fundamental Theorems & Invariants

- **Additive Independence & Chebyshev Transform:** In $\ell_1$ (Manhattan) and $\ell_\infty$ (Chebyshev) metrics, coordinate transformations $(x', y') = (x+y, x-y)$ decouple multi-dimensional distance minimization into independent 1D median problems.
- **Slope Trick Invariant:** Any continuous piecewise linear convex function $f(x)$ with integer slopes can be represented by its minimum value and two priority queues storing the inflection points where the slope changes by $+1$ and $-1$. Adding $|x - a|$ updates slope inflection points in $\mathcal{O}(\log N)$.
- **Alien's Trick / WQS Binary Search:** If the optimal value $OPT(k)$ as a function of choice count $k$ is strictly concave/convex, introducing a Lagrange multiplier penalty $\lambda$ removes the exact-count constraint, binary searching $\lambda$ in $\mathcal{O}(\text{BaseDP} \cdot \log(\text{Range})))$.

##### Algorithmic Execution Blueprint

1. **Chebyshev Transformation:** Map each point $(x, y) \to (x+y, x-y)$. The maximum Chebyshev distance $\max(|x_1-x_2|, |y_1-y_2|)$ becomes $\frac{1}{2}(|x'_1-x'_2| + |y'_1-y'_2|)$.
2. **Slope Trick Maintenance:** Maintain left heap $L$ (max-heap) and right heap $R$ (min-heap). To add $|x - a|$: if $a < L.\text{top}()$, add cost $L.\text{top}() - a$, push $a$ twice to $L$, and move $L.\text{top}()$ to $R$.
3. **WQS Binary Search:** Binary search penalty $\lambda$. Run unconstrained DP where each selection costs $+\lambda$. If optimal selections count $> k$, increase $\lambda$.

##### Closed-Form Invariant Equations

$$f_{new}(x) = f(x) + |x - a|, \quad \text{WQS}: \min_k \big(dp[k] - \lambda k\big) \iff \text{Lagrangian Duality}$$

##### Structural Edge Cases & Assumptions

- Slope Trick requires the underlying objective function to be strictly convex; non-convex cost functions require general CHT or DP.
- In WQS Binary Search, collinear points (flat segments in the convex hull of $OPT(k)$) require careful tie-breaking (preferring max count or min count) to avoid binary search oscillation.

---

#### 3. Core Intuition & Problem Framing

1. In your `check(lambda)` function, when two choices have the same cost, consistently break ties by choosing the **maximum count** (or minimum count).
2. Because the points are collinear, the true value $F(K)$ lies on the straight line connecting $(k_{\min}, F(k_{\min}))$ and $(k_{\max}, F(k_{\max}))$.
3. The exact same recovery formula still holds:
   $$F(K) = g(\lambda) - \lambda \cdot K$$
   You **do not need to reconstruct the exact configuration of $K$ items**—the formula computes the exact mathematical value $F(K)$ on the line segment!

---

## 3. Reusable C++ WQS Template

```cpp
// C++17 — WQS Binary Search (Alien's Trick) Template
#include <bits/stdc++.h>
using namespace std;

struct Result {
    long long cost;
    int count;
};

// Solve unconstrained problem with penalty lambda per chosen element
Result check(long long lambda, const vector<long long>& a) {
    int n = a.size();
    // dp[i] = {min_cost, min_count_used}
    vector<pair<long long, int>> dp(n + 1, {0, 0});
    
    for (int i = 1; i <= n; ++i) {
        // Option 1: Do not pick
        dp[i] = dp[i - 1];
        
        // Option 2: Pick item i (costs a[i-1] + lambda)
        long long take_cost = dp[i - 1].first + a[i - 1] + lambda;
        int take_cnt = dp[i - 1].second + 1;
        
        if (take_cost < dp[i].first || (take_cost == dp[i].first && take_cnt > dp[i].second)) {
            dp[i] = {take_cost, take_cnt};
        }
    }
    return {dp[n].first, dp[n].second};
}

long long wqs_solve(int n, int K, const vector<long long>& a) {
    long long low = -1e14, high = 1e14;
    long long best_lambda = 0;
    
    while (low <= high) {
        long long mid = low + (high - low) / 2;
        Result res = check(mid, a);
        
        if (res.count >= K) {
            best_lambda = mid;
            low = mid + 1; // Increase penalty to reduce count
        } else {
            high = mid - 1; // Decrease penalty to increase count
        }
    }
    
    Result final_res = check(best_lambda, a);
    return final_res.cost - best_lambda * K;
}
```

---

```cpp
        }
    }
    return {dp[n].first, dp[n].second};
}

long long wqs_solve(int n, int K, const vector<long long>& a) {
```

### 51. Python Implementation: The Flat Array Trie

> **Section:** §20 Greedy Intermediate (Slope Trick, WQS)  
> **Phase:** Phase 5 — Dynamic Programming & Advanced Greedy  
> **Contest ROI:** B-tier | **Rating Range:** 1600–2200 | **Frequency:** Common

#### 2. Theoretical Deep-Dive & Mathematical Mechanics

##### Overview

Intermediate Greedy techniques bridge convex optimization and dynamic programming: Decoupled Dimension Sweeps, Slope Trick (piecewise linear convex function maintenance via priority queues), and WQS Binary Search (Alien's Trick).

##### Fundamental Theorems & Invariants

- **Additive Independence & Chebyshev Transform:** In $\ell_1$ (Manhattan) and $\ell_\infty$ (Chebyshev) metrics, coordinate transformations $(x', y') = (x+y, x-y)$ decouple multi-dimensional distance minimization into independent 1D median problems.
- **Slope Trick Invariant:** Any continuous piecewise linear convex function $f(x)$ with integer slopes can be represented by its minimum value and two priority queues storing the inflection points where the slope changes by $+1$ and $-1$. Adding $|x - a|$ updates slope inflection points in $\mathcal{O}(\log N)$.
- **Alien's Trick / WQS Binary Search:** If the optimal value $OPT(k)$ as a function of choice count $k$ is strictly concave/convex, introducing a Lagrange multiplier penalty $\lambda$ removes the exact-count constraint, binary searching $\lambda$ in $\mathcal{O}(\text{BaseDP} \cdot \log(\text{Range})))$.

##### Algorithmic Execution Blueprint

1. **Chebyshev Transformation:** Map each point $(x, y) \to (x+y, x-y)$. The maximum Chebyshev distance $\max(|x_1-x_2|, |y_1-y_2|)$ becomes $\frac{1}{2}(|x'_1-x'_2| + |y'_1-y'_2|)$.
2. **Slope Trick Maintenance:** Maintain left heap $L$ (max-heap) and right heap $R$ (min-heap). To add $|x - a|$: if $a < L.\text{top}()$, add cost $L.\text{top}() - a$, push $a$ twice to $L$, and move $L.\text{top}()$ to $R$.
3. **WQS Binary Search:** Binary search penalty $\lambda$. Run unconstrained DP where each selection costs $+\lambda$. If optimal selections count $> k$, increase $\lambda$.

##### Closed-Form Invariant Equations

$$f_{new}(x) = f(x) + |x - a|, \quad \text{WQS}: \min_k \big(dp[k] - \lambda k\big) \iff \text{Lagrangian Duality}$$

##### Structural Edge Cases & Assumptions

- Slope Trick requires the underlying objective function to be strictly convex; non-convex cost functions require general CHT or DP.

---

#### 5. Contest-Ready Implementation Templates

##### Python 3 Template

```python
class BinaryTrie:
    def __init__(self, max_queries, bit_len=30):
        # max_nodes = (number of insertions) * bit_len + 2
        # trie[node][0] points to the left child (bit 0)
        # trie[node][1] points to the right child (bit 1)
        self.bit_len = bit_len
        self.max_nodes = max_queries * bit_len + 2
        self.trie = [[0, 0] for _ in range(self.max_nodes)]
        self.node_count = 1  # Root is at index 1
        
    def insert(self, num):
        curr = 1
        for i in range(self.bit_len - 1, -1, -1):
            bit = (num >> i) & 1
            if self.trie[curr][bit] == 0:
                self.node_count += 1
                self.trie[curr][bit] = self.node_count
            curr = self.trie[curr][bit]
            
    def query_max_xor(self, x):
        curr = 1
        max_xor = 0
        for i in range(self.bit_len - 1, -1, -1):
            bit = (x >> i) & 1
            desired_bit = bit ^ 1  # We want the opposite bit
            
            # Greedily take the opposite bit if the branch exists
            if self.trie[curr][desired_bit] != 0:
                max_xor |= (1 << i)
                curr = self.trie[curr][desired_bit]
            else:
                # Forced to take the same bit
                curr = self.trie[curr][bit]
                
        return max_xor
```

2. **Slope Trick Maintenance:** Maintain left heap $L$ (max-heap) and right heap $R$ (min-heap). To add $|x - a|$: if $a < L.\text{top}()$, add cost $L.\text{top}() - a$, push $a$ twice to $L$, and move $L.\text{top}()$ to $R$.
3. **WQS Binary Search:** Binary search penalty $\lambda$. Run unconstrained DP where each selection costs $+\lambda$. If optimal selections count $> k$, increase $\lambda$.

##### Closed-Form Invariant Equations

$$f_{new}(x) = f(x) + |x - a|, \quad \text{WQS}: \min_k \big(dp[k] - \lambda k\big) \iff \text{Lagrangian Duality}$$

##### Structural Edge Cases & Assumptions

- Slope Trick requires the underlying objective function to be strictly convex; non-convex cost functions require general CHT or DP.
- In WQS Binary Search, collinear points (flat segments in the convex hull of $OPT(k)$) require careful tie-breaking (preferring max count or min count) to avoid binary search oscillation.

<a id="topic-53"></a>
### 53. Maintaining Subtree Sizes & Dynamic MEX

> **Section:** §20 Greedy Intermediate (Slope Trick, WQS)  
> **Phase:** Phase 5 — Dynamic Programming & Advanced Greedy  
> **Contest ROI:** B-tier | **Rating Range:** 1600–2200 | **Frequency:** Common  

---

#### 2. Theoretical Deep-Dive & Mathematical Mechanics

##### Overview

Intermediate Greedy techniques bridge convex optimization and dynamic programming: Decoupled Dimension Sweeps, Slope Trick (piecewise linear convex function maintenance via priority queues), and WQS Binary Search (Alien's Trick).

##### Fundamental Theorems & Invariants

- **Additive Independence & Chebyshev Transform:** In $\ell_1$ (Manhattan) and $\ell_\infty$ (Chebyshev) metrics, coordinate transformations $(x', y') = (x+y, x-y)$ decouple multi-dimensional distance minimization into independent 1D median problems.
- **Slope Trick Invariant:** Any continuous piecewise linear convex function $f(x)$ with integer slopes can be represented by its minimum value and two priority queues storing the inflection points where the slope changes by $+1$ and $-1$. Adding $|x - a|$ updates slope inflection points in $\mathcal{O}(\log N)$.
- **Alien's Trick / WQS Binary Search:** If the optimal value $OPT(k)$ as a function of choice count $k$ is strictly concave/convex, introducing a Lagrange multiplier penalty $\lambda$ removes the exact-count constraint, binary searching $\lambda$ in $\mathcal{O}(\text{BaseDP} \cdot \log(\text{Range})))$.

##### Algorithmic Execution Blueprint

1. **Chebyshev Transformation:** Map each point $(x, y) \to (x+y, x-y)$. The maximum Chebyshev distance $\max(|x_1-x_2|, |y_1-y_2|)$ becomes $\frac{1}{2}(|x'_1-x'_2| + |y'_1-y'_2|)$.
2. **Slope Trick Maintenance:** Maintain left heap $L$ (max-heap) and right heap $R$ (min-heap). To add $|x - a|$: if $a < L.\text{top}()$, add cost $L.\text{top}() - a$, push $a$ twice to $L$, and move $L.\text{top}()$ to $R$.
3. **WQS Binary Search:** Binary search penalty $\lambda$. Run unconstrained DP where each selection costs $+\lambda$. If optimal selections count $> k$, increase $\lambda$.

##### Closed-Form Invariant Equations

$$f_{new}(x) = f(x) + |x - a|, \quad \text{WQS}: \min_k \big(dp[k] - \lambda k\big) \iff \text{Lagrangian Duality}$$

##### Structural Edge Cases & Assumptions

- Slope Trick requires the underlying objective function to be strictly convex; non-convex cost functions require general CHT or DP.
- In WQS Binary Search, collinear points (flat segments in the convex hull of $OPT(k)$) require careful tie-breaking (preferring max count or min count) to avoid binary search oscillation.

### 55. Python Implementation: The Walkable SegTree

> **Section:** §20 Greedy Intermediate (Slope Trick, WQS)  
> **Phase:** Phase 5 — Dynamic Programming & Advanced Greedy  
> **Contest ROI:** B-tier | **Rating Range:** 1600–2200 | **Frequency:** Common  

---

#### 2. Theoretical Deep-Dive & Mathematical Mechanics

##### Overview

Intermediate Greedy techniques bridge convex optimization and dynamic programming: Decoupled Dimension Sweeps, Slope Trick (piecewise linear convex function maintenance via priority queues), and WQS Binary Search (Alien's Trick).

##### Fundamental Theorems & Invariants

- **Additive Independence & Chebyshev Transform:** In $\ell_1$ (Manhattan) and $\ell_\infty$ (Chebyshev) metrics, coordinate transformations $(x', y') = (x+y, x-y)$ decouple multi-dimensional distance minimization into independent 1D median problems.
- **Slope Trick Invariant:** Any continuous piecewise linear convex function $f(x)$ with integer slopes can be represented by its minimum value and two priority queues storing the inflection points where the slope changes by $+1$ and $-1$. Adding $|x - a|$ updates slope inflection points in $\mathcal{O}(\log N)$.
- **Alien's Trick / WQS Binary Search:** If the optimal value $OPT(k)$ as a function of choice count $k$ is strictly concave/convex, introducing a Lagrange multiplier penalty $\lambda$ removes the exact-count constraint, binary searching $\lambda$ in $\mathcal{O}(\text{BaseDP} \cdot \log(\text{Range})))$.

##### Algorithmic Execution Blueprint

1. **Chebyshev Transformation:** Map each point $(x, y) \to (x+y, x-y)$. The maximum Chebyshev distance $\max(|x_1-x_2|, |y_1-y_2|)$ becomes $\frac{1}{2}(|x'_1-x'_2| + |y'_1-y'_2|)$.
2. **Slope Trick Maintenance:** Maintain left heap $L$ (max-heap) and right heap $R$ (min-heap). To add $|x - a|$: if $a < L.\text{top}()$, add cost $L.\text{top}() - a$, push $a$ twice to $L$, and move $L.\text{top}()$ to $R$.
3. **WQS Binary Search:** Binary search penalty $\lambda$. Run unconstrained DP where each selection costs $+\lambda$. If optimal selections count $> k$, increase $\lambda$.

##### Closed-Form Invariant Equations

$$f_{new}(x) = f(x) + |x - a|, \quad \text{WQS}: \min_k \big(dp[k] - \lambda k\big) \iff \text{Lagrangian Duality}$$

##### Structural Edge Cases & Assumptions

- Slope Trick requires the underlying objective function to be strictly convex; non-convex cost functions require general CHT or DP.

---

#### 5. Contest-Ready Implementation Templates

##### Python 3 Template

```python
class WalkableSegTree:
    def __init__(self, n, data):
        self.n = n
        self.tree = [0] * (4 * n)
        self.build(data, 1, 0, n - 1)

    def build(self, data, v, tl, tr):
        if tl == tr:
            self.tree[v] = data[tl]
        else:
            tm = (tl + tr) // 2
            self.build(data, v * 2, tl, tm)
            self.build(data, v * 2 + 1, tm + 1, tr)
            self.tree[v] = max(self.tree[v * 2], self.tree[v * 2 + 1])

    def update(self, v, tl, tr, pos, new_val):
        """Standard Point Update"""
        if tl == tr:
            self.tree[v] = new_val
        else:
            tm = (tl + tr) // 2
            if pos <= tm:
                self.update(v * 2, tl, tm, pos, new_val)
            else:
                self.update(v * 2 + 1, tm + 1, tr, pos, new_val)
            self.tree[v] = max(self.tree[v * 2], self.tree[v * 2 + 1])

    def find_first(self, v, tl, tr, x):
        """Finds the first index i in [0, N-1] where A[i] >= x"""
        if self.tree[v] < x:
            return -1
        if tl == tr:
            return tl
            
        tm = (tl + tr) // 2
        if self.tree[v * 2] >= x:
            return self.find_first(v * 2, tl, tm, x)
        else:
            return self.find_first(v * 2 + 1, tm + 1, tr, x)
```

---

#### 5. Contest-Ready Implementation Templates

##### Python 3 Template

```python
def find_first_from(self, v, tl, tr, l, r, x):
        """Finds the first index i in [l, r] where A[i] >= x"""
        if tl > r or tr < l or self.tree[v] < x:
            return -1
        if tl == tr:
            return tl
            
        tm = (tl + tr) // 2
        # Greedily try the left side first (if it intersects our target range)
        res = self.find_first_from(v * 2, tl, tm, l, r, x)
        if res != -1:
            return res
        return self.find_first_from(v * 2 + 1, tm + 1, tr, l, r, x)
```

- In WQS Binary Search, collinear points (flat segments in the convex hull of $OPT(k)$) require careful tie-breaking (preferring max count or min count) to avoid binary search oscillation.
