<a id="topic-59"></a>
### 59. The CP Translation: Intervals on a Line

> **Section:** §20 Greedy Intermediate (Slope Trick, WQS)  
> **Phase:** Phase 5 — Dynamic Programming & Advanced Greedy  
> **Contest ROI:** B-tier | **Rating Range:** 1600–2200 | **Frequency:** Common  

---

#### 2. Theoretical Deep-Dive & Mathematical Mechanics

##### Overview

Intermediate Greedy techniques bridge convex optimization and dynamic programming: Decoupled Dimension Sweeps, Slope Trick (piecewise linear convex function maintenance via priority queues), and WQS Binary Search (Alien's Trick).

##### Fundamental Theorems & Invariants

- **Additive Independence & Chebyshev Transform:** In $\ell_1$ (Manhattan) and $\ell_\infty$ (Chebyshev) metrics, coordinate transformations $(x', y') = (x+y, x-y)$ decouple multi-dimensional distance minimization into independent 1D median problems.
- **Slope Trick Invariant:** Any continuous piecewise linear convex function $f(x)$ with integer slopes can be represented by its minimum value and two priority queues storing the inflection points where the slope changes by $+1$ and $-1$. Adding $|x - a|$ updates slope inflection points in $\mathcal{O}(\log N)$.
- **Alien's Trick / WQS Binary Search:** If the optimal value $OPT(k)$ as a function of choice count $k$ is strictly concave/convex, introducing a Lagrange multiplier penalty $\lambda$ removes the exact-count constraint, binary searching $\lambda$ in $\mathcal{O}(\text{BaseDP} \cdot \log(\text{Range})))$.

##### Algorithmic Execution Blueprint

1. **Chebyshev Transformation:** Map each point $(x, y) \to (x+y, x-y)$. The maximum Chebyshev distance $\max(|x_1-x_2|, |y_1-y_2|)$ becomes $\frac{1}{2}(|x'_1-x'_2| + |y'_1-y'_2|)$.
2. **Slope Trick Maintenance:** Maintain left heap $L$ (max-heap) and right heap $R$ (min-heap). To add $|x - a|$: if $a < L.\text{top}()$, add cost $L.\text{top}() - a$, push $a$ twice to $L$, and move $L.\text{top}()$ to $R$.
3. **WQS Binary Search:** Binary search penalty $\lambda$. Run unconstrained DP where each selection costs $+\lambda$. If optimal selections count $> k$, increase $\lambda$.

##### Closed-Form Invariant Equations

$$f_{new}(x) = f(x) + |x - a|, \quad \text{WQS}: \min_k \big(dp[k] - \lambda k\big) \iff \text{Lagrangian Duality}$$

##### Structural Edge Cases & Assumptions

- Slope Trick requires the underlying objective function to be strictly convex; non-convex cost functions require general CHT or DP.
- In WQS Binary Search, collinear points (flat segments in the convex hull of $OPT(k)$) require careful tie-breaking (preferring max count or min count) to avoid binary search oscillation.

### 61. Python Implementation: The Lazy Segment Tree Oracle

> **Section:** §20 Greedy Intermediate (Slope Trick, WQS)  
> **Phase:** Phase 5 — Dynamic Programming & Advanced Greedy  
> **Contest ROI:** B-tier | **Rating Range:** 1600–2200 | **Frequency:** Common  

---

#### 2. Theoretical Deep-Dive & Mathematical Mechanics

##### Overview

Intermediate Greedy techniques bridge convex optimization and dynamic programming: Decoupled Dimension Sweeps, Slope Trick (piecewise linear convex function maintenance via priority queues), and WQS Binary Search (Alien's Trick).

##### Fundamental Theorems & Invariants

- **Additive Independence & Chebyshev Transform:** In $\ell_1$ (Manhattan) and $\ell_\infty$ (Chebyshev) metrics, coordinate transformations $(x', y') = (x+y, x-y)$ decouple multi-dimensional distance minimization into independent 1D median problems.
- **Slope Trick Invariant:** Any continuous piecewise linear convex function $f(x)$ with integer slopes can be represented by its minimum value and two priority queues storing the inflection points where the slope changes by $+1$ and $-1$. Adding $|x - a|$ updates slope inflection points in $\mathcal{O}(\log N)$.
- **Alien's Trick / WQS Binary Search:** If the optimal value $OPT(k)$ as a function of choice count $k$ is strictly concave/convex, introducing a Lagrange multiplier penalty $\lambda$ removes the exact-count constraint, binary searching $\lambda$ in $\mathcal{O}(\text{BaseDP} \cdot \log(\text{Range})))$.

##### Algorithmic Execution Blueprint

1. **Chebyshev Transformation:** Map each point $(x, y) \to (x+y, x-y)$. The maximum Chebyshev distance $\max(|x_1-x_2|, |y_1-y_2|)$ becomes $\frac{1}{2}(|x'_1-x'_2| + |y'_1-y'_2|)$.
2. **Slope Trick Maintenance:** Maintain left heap $L$ (max-heap) and right heap $R$ (min-heap). To add $|x - a|$: if $a < L.\text{top}()$, add cost $L.\text{top}() - a$, push $a$ twice to $L$, and move $L.\text{top}()$ to $R$.
3. **WQS Binary Search:** Binary search penalty $\lambda$. Run unconstrained DP where each selection costs $+\lambda$. If optimal selections count $> k$, increase $\lambda$.

##### Closed-Form Invariant Equations

$$f_{new}(x) = f(x) + |x - a|, \quad \text{WQS}: \min_k \big(dp[k] - \lambda k\big) \iff \text{Lagrangian Duality}$$

##### Structural Edge Cases & Assumptions

- Slope Trick requires the underlying objective function to be strictly convex; non-convex cost functions require general CHT or DP.
- In WQS Binary Search, collinear points (flat segments in the convex hull of $OPT(k)$) require careful tie-breaking (preferring max count or min count) to avoid binary search oscillation.

---

#### 5. Contest-Ready Implementation Templates

##### Python 3 Template

```python
class HallsOracle:
    def __init__(self, max_val):
        self.n = max_val
        self.tree = [0] * (4 * self.n)
        self.lazy = [0] * (4 * self.n)
        self.build(1, 0, self.n - 1)
        
    def build(self, v, tl, tr):
        if tl == tr:
            self.tree[v] = tl + 1
        else:
            tm = (tl + tr) // 2
            self.build(v * 2, tl, tm)
            self.build(v * 2 + 1, tm + 1, tr)
            self.tree[v] = min(self.tree[v * 2], self.tree[v * 2 + 1])
            
    def push(self, v):
        if self.lazy[v] != 0:
            self.tree[v * 2] += self.lazy[v]
            self.lazy[v * 2] += self.lazy[v]
            self.tree[v * 2 + 1] += self.lazy[v]
            self.lazy[v * 2 + 1] += self.lazy[v]
            self.lazy[v] = 0
            
    def add(self, v, tl, tr, l, r, val):
        """Adds val to range [l, r]. If val is -1, it consumes capacity."""
        if l > r: return
        if l == tl and r == tr:
            self.tree[v] += val
            self.lazy[v] += val
        else:
            self.push(v)
            tm = (tl + tr) // 2
            self.add(v * 2, tl, tm, l, min(r, tm), val)
            self.add(v * 2 + 1, tm + 1, tr, max(l, tm + 1), r, val)
            self.tree[v] = min(self.tree[v * 2], self.tree[v * 2 + 1])

    def is_valid(self):
        """If the minimum slack is >= 0, Hall's Condition is satisfied."""
        return self.tree[1] >= 0
```

### 64. Python Implementation: MEX & Grundy DP

> **Section:** §20 Greedy Intermediate (Slope Trick, WQS)  
> **Phase:** Phase 5 — Dynamic Programming & Advanced Greedy  
> **Contest ROI:** B-tier | **Rating Range:** 1600–2200 | **Frequency:** Common  

---

#### 2. Theoretical Deep-Dive & Mathematical Mechanics

##### Overview

Intermediate Greedy techniques bridge convex optimization and dynamic programming: Decoupled Dimension Sweeps, Slope Trick (piecewise linear convex function maintenance via priority queues), and WQS Binary Search (Alien's Trick).

##### Fundamental Theorems & Invariants

- **Additive Independence & Chebyshev Transform:** In $\ell_1$ (Manhattan) and $\ell_\infty$ (Chebyshev) metrics, coordinate transformations $(x', y') = (x+y, x-y)$ decouple multi-dimensional distance minimization into independent 1D median problems.
- **Slope Trick Invariant:** Any continuous piecewise linear convex function $f(x)$ with integer slopes can be represented by its minimum value and two priority queues storing the inflection points where the slope changes by $+1$ and $-1$. Adding $|x - a|$ updates slope inflection points in $\mathcal{O}(\log N)$.
- **Alien's Trick / WQS Binary Search:** If the optimal value $OPT(k)$ as a function of choice count $k$ is strictly concave/convex, introducing a Lagrange multiplier penalty $\lambda$ removes the exact-count constraint, binary searching $\lambda$ in $\mathcal{O}(\text{BaseDP} \cdot \log(\text{Range})))$.

##### Algorithmic Execution Blueprint

1. **Chebyshev Transformation:** Map each point $(x, y) \to (x+y, x-y)$. The maximum Chebyshev distance $\max(|x_1-x_2|, |y_1-y_2|)$ becomes $\frac{1}{2}(|x'_1-x'_2| + |y'_1-y'_2|)$.
2. **Slope Trick Maintenance:** Maintain left heap $L$ (max-heap) and right heap $R$ (min-heap). To add $|x - a|$: if $a < L.\text{top}()$, add cost $L.\text{top}() - a$, push $a$ twice to $L$, and move $L.\text{top}()$ to $R$.
3. **WQS Binary Search:** Binary search penalty $\lambda$. Run unconstrained DP where each selection costs $+\lambda$. If optimal selections count $> k$, increase $\lambda$.

---

#### 5. Contest-Ready Implementation Templates

##### Python 3 Template

```python
def solve_grundy(n, allowed_moves):
    G = [-1] * (n + 1)
    G[0] = 0  # Terminal state: 0 moves
    
    for state in range(1, n + 1):
        seen_grundy_values = set()
        for move in allowed_moves:
            if state - move >= 0:
                seen_grundy_values.add(G[state - move])
                
        # Calculate MEX
        mex = 0
        while mex in seen_grundy_values:
            mex += 1
        G[state] = mex
        
    return G
```

<a id="topic-65"></a>
### 65. The Subgame Split Rule

> **Section:** §20 Greedy Intermediate (Slope Trick, WQS)  
> **Phase:** Phase 5 — Dynamic Programming & Advanced Greedy  
> **Contest ROI:** B-tier | **Rating Range:** 1600–2200 | **Frequency:** Common  

---

#### 2. Theoretical Deep-Dive & Mathematical Mechanics

##### Overview

Intermediate Greedy techniques bridge convex optimization and dynamic programming: Decoupled Dimension Sweeps, Slope Trick (piecewise linear convex function maintenance via priority queues), and WQS Binary Search (Alien's Trick).

##### Fundamental Theorems & Invariants

- **Additive Independence & Chebyshev Transform:** In $\ell_1$ (Manhattan) and $\ell_\infty$ (Chebyshev) metrics, coordinate transformations $(x', y') = (x+y, x-y)$ decouple multi-dimensional distance minimization into independent 1D median problems.
- **Slope Trick Invariant:** Any continuous piecewise linear convex function $f(x)$ with integer slopes can be represented by its minimum value and two priority queues storing the inflection points where the slope changes by $+1$ and $-1$. Adding $|x - a|$ updates slope inflection points in $\mathcal{O}(\log N)$.
- **Alien's Trick / WQS Binary Search:** If the optimal value $OPT(k)$ as a function of choice count $k$ is strictly concave/convex, introducing a Lagrange multiplier penalty $\lambda$ removes the exact-count constraint, binary searching $\lambda$ in $\mathcal{O}(\text{BaseDP} \cdot \log(\text{Range})))$.

##### Algorithmic Execution Blueprint

1. **Chebyshev Transformation:** Map each point $(x, y) \to (x+y, x-y)$. The maximum Chebyshev distance $\max(|x_1-x_2|, |y_1-y_2|)$ becomes $\frac{1}{2}(|x'_1-x'_2| + |y'_1-y'_2|)$.
2. **Slope Trick Maintenance:** Maintain left heap $L$ (max-heap) and right heap $R$ (min-heap). To add $|x - a|$: if $a < L.\text{top}()$, add cost $L.\text{top}() - a$, push $a$ twice to $L$, and move $L.\text{top}()$ to $R$.
3. **WQS Binary Search:** Binary search penalty $\lambda$. Run unconstrained DP where each selection costs $+\lambda$. If optimal selections count $> k$, increase $\lambda$.

##### Closed-Form Invariant Equations

$$f_{new}(x) = f(x) + |x - a|, \quad \text{WQS}: \min_k \big(dp[k] - \lambda k\big) \iff \text{Lagrangian Duality}$$

##### Structural Edge Cases & Assumptions

- Slope Trick requires the underlying objective function to be strictly convex; non-convex cost functions require general CHT or DP.
- In WQS Binary Search, collinear points (flat segments in the convex hull of $OPT(k)$) require careful tie-breaking (preferring max count or min count) to avoid binary search oscillation.

---

<a id="topic-67"></a>
### 67. What is Slope Trick?

> **Section:** §20 Greedy Intermediate (Slope Trick, WQS)  
> **Phase:** Phase 5 — Dynamic Programming & Advanced Greedy  
> **Contest ROI:** S-tier (Turns $O(N^2)$ DP into $O(N \log N)$) | **Rating Range:** 1600–2200 | **Frequency:** Classic in Codeforces 2000–2600+  

---

#### 1. Contest Payoff & Why It Matters

- Contest ROI: S-tier (Turns $O(N^2)$ DP into $O(N \log N)$)
- Frequency: Classic in Codeforces 2000–2600+
- Core Concept: Tracking the **derivative / slope transitions** of a dynamic cost function rather than tabular values.

---

#### 2. Theoretical Deep-Dive & Mathematical Mechanics

##### Overview

Intermediate Greedy techniques bridge convex optimization and dynamic programming: Decoupled Dimension Sweeps, Slope Trick (piecewise linear convex function maintenance via priority queues), and WQS Binary Search (Alien's Trick).

##### Fundamental Theorems & Invariants

- **Additive Independence & Chebyshev Transform:** In $\ell_1$ (Manhattan) and $\ell_\infty$ (Chebyshev) metrics, coordinate transformations $(x', y') = (x+y, x-y)$ decouple multi-dimensional distance minimization into independent 1D median problems.
- **Slope Trick Invariant:** Any continuous piecewise linear convex function $f(x)$ with integer slopes can be represented by its minimum value and two priority queues storing the inflection points where the slope changes by $+1$ and $-1$. Adding $|x - a|$ updates slope inflection points in $\mathcal{O}(\log N)$.
- **Alien's Trick / WQS Binary Search:** If the optimal value $OPT(k)$ as a function of choice count $k$ is strictly concave/convex, introducing a Lagrange multiplier penalty $\lambda$ removes the exact-count constraint, binary searching $\lambda$ in $\mathcal{O}(\text{BaseDP} \cdot \log(\text{Range})))$.

##### Algorithmic Execution Blueprint

1. **Chebyshev Transformation:** Map each point $(x, y) \to (x+y, x-y)$. The maximum Chebyshev distance $\max(|x_1-x_2|, |y_1-y_2|)$ becomes $\frac{1}{2}(|x'_1-x'_2| + |y'_1-y'_2|)$.
2. **Slope Trick Maintenance:** Maintain left heap $L$ (max-heap) and right heap $R$ (min-heap). To add $|x - a|$: if $a < L.\text{top}()$, add cost $L.\text{top}() - a$, push $a$ twice to $L$, and move $L.\text{top}()$ to $R$.
3. **WQS Binary Search:** Binary search penalty $\lambda$. Run unconstrained DP where each selection costs $+\lambda$. If optimal selections count $> k$, increase $\lambda$.

##### Closed-Form Invariant Equations

$$f_{new}(x) = f(x) + |x - a|, \quad \text{WQS}: \min_k \big(dp[k] - \lambda k\big) \iff \text{Lagrangian Duality}$$

##### Structural Edge Cases & Assumptions

- Slope Trick requires the underlying objective function to be strictly convex; non-convex cost functions require general CHT or DP.
- In WQS Binary Search, collinear points (flat segments in the convex hull of $OPT(k)$) require careful tie-breaking (preferring max count or min count) to avoid binary search oscillation.

---

#### 3. Core Intuition & Problem Framing

A function $f: \mathbb{R} \to \mathbb{R}$ is **continuous, piecewise linear, and convex** if:
1. It is composed of linear segments with integer slopes: $\dots, -3, -2, -1, 0, 1, 2, 3, \dots$.
2. The derivative (slope) $f'(x)$ is a **monotone non-decreasing step function**.
3. Because $f'(x)$ is non-decreasing, it is completely defined by the **locations of its step increases**.
4. By the Fundamental Theorem of Calculus:
   $$f(x) = f(x_0) + \int_{x_0}^x f'(t) \, dt$$
   Knowing the minimum value $f_{\min}$, the interval $[L, R]$ where $f'(x) = 0$, and the multiset of slope change locations completely determines the entire function $f(x)$ for all $x \in \mathbb{R}$!

---

#### 6. Recognition Trigger Signals & Heuristics

- `L` (Max-Heap):** Contains points where the slope changes in $(-\infty, \text{min\_range\_left}]$. These define slopes $-1, -2, -3, \dots$ moving leftward.
- `R` (Min-Heap):** Contains points where the slope changes in $[\text{min\_range\_right}, \infty)$. These define slopes $+1, +2, +3, \dots$ moving rightward.
- `min_f`:** A scalar maintaining the minimum value $\min_{x} f(x)$.
- `off_L`, `off_R`:** Lazy horizontal shift offsets applied to all elements in $L$ and $R$.
-

##### Overview

Intermediate Greedy techniques bridge convex optimization and dynamic programming: Decoupled Dimension Sweeps, Slope Trick (piecewise linear convex function maintenance via priority queues), and WQS Binary Search (Alien's Trick).

##### Fundamental Theorems & Invariants

- **Additive Independence & Chebyshev Transform:** In $\ell_1$ (Manhattan) and $\ell_\infty$ (Chebyshev) metrics, coordinate transformations $(x', y') = (x+y, x-y)$ decouple multi-dimensional distance minimization into independent 1D median problems.
- **Slope Trick Invariant:** Any continuous piecewise linear convex function $f(x)$ with integer slopes can be represented by its minimum value and two priority queues storing the inflection points where the slope changes by $+1$ and $-1$. Adding $|x - a|$ updates slope inflection points in $\mathcal{O}(\log N)$.
- **Alien's Trick / WQS Binary Search:** If the optimal value $OPT(k)$ as a function of choice count $k$ is strictly concave/convex, introducing a Lagrange multiplier penalty $\lambda$ removes the exact-count constraint, binary searching $\lambda$ in $\mathcal{O}(\text{BaseDP} \cdot \log(\text{Range})))$.

##### Algorithmic Execution Blueprint

1. **Chebyshev Transformation:** Map each point $(x, y) \to (x+y, x-y)$. The maximum Chebyshev distance $\max(|x_1-x_2|, |y_1-y_2|)$ becomes $\frac{1}{2}(|x'_1-x'_2| + |y'_1-y'_2|)$.
2. **Slope Trick Maintenance:** Maintain left heap $L$ (max-heap) and right heap $R$ (min-heap). To add $|x - a|$: if $a < L.\text{top}()$, add cost $L.\text{top}() - a$, push $a$ twice to $L$, and move $L.\text{top}()$ to $R$.
3. **WQS Binary Search:** Binary search penalty $\lambda$. Run unconstrained DP where each selection costs $+\lambda$. If optimal selections count $> k$, increase $\lambda$.

##### Closed-Form Invariant Equations

$$f_{new}(x) = f(x) + |x - a|, \quad \text{WQS}: \min_k \big(dp[k] - \lambda k\big) \iff \text{Lagrangian Duality}$$

##### Structural Edge Cases & Assumptions

- Slope Trick requires the underlying objective function to be strictly convex; non-convex cost functions require general CHT or DP.
- In WQS Binary Search, collinear points (flat segments in the convex hull of $OPT(k)$) require careful tie-breaking (preferring max count or min count) to avoid binary search oscillation.

---

#### 5. Contest-Ready Implementation Templates

##### C++17 Template

```cpp
// C++17 — Complete Slope Trick Class
#include <bits/stdc++.h>
using namespace std;

class SlopeTrick {
public:
    long long min_f = 0;
    long long off_L = 0, off_R = 0;
    priority_queue<long long> L; // Max-heap
    priority_queue<long long, vector<long long>, greater<long long>> R; // Min-heap

    // Adds |x - a| to f(x)
    void add_abs(long long a) {
        long long l_max = L.empty() ? -1e18 : (L.top() + off_L);
        long long r_min = R.empty() ? 1e18 : (R.top() + off_R);

        if (a < l_max) {
            min_f += (l_max - a);
            L.push(a - off_L);
            L.push(a - off_L);
            long long move_val = L.top() + off_L;
            L.pop();
            R.push(move_val - off_R);
        } else if (a > r_min) {
            min_f += (a - r_min);
            R.push(a - off_R);
            R.push(a - off_R);
            long long move_val = R.top() + off_R;
            R.pop();
            L.push(move_val - off_L);
        } else {
            L.push(a - off_L);
            R.push(a - off_R);
        }
    }

    // Adds max(0, a - x) (only left-sloping penalty)
    void add_left_penalty(long long a) {
        long long r_min = R.empty() ? 1e18 : (R.top() + off_R);
        if (a > r_min) {
            min_f += (a - r_min);
            R.push(a - off_R);
            long long move_val = R.top() + off_R;
            R.pop();
            L.push(move_val - off_L);
        } else {
            L.push(a - off_L);
        }
    }

    // Adds max(0, x - a) (only right-sloping penalty)
    void add_right_penalty(long long a) {
        long long l_max = L.empty() ? -1e18 : (L.top() + off_L);
        if (a < l_max) {
            min_f += (l_max - a);
            L.push(a - off_L);
            long long move_val = L.top() + off_L;
            L.pop();
            R.push(move_val - off_R);
        } else {
            R.push(a - off_R);
        }
    }

    // Prefix minimum: g(x) = min_{y <= x} f(y)
    void prefix_min() {
        while (!R.empty()) R.pop();
    }

    // Suffix minimum: g(x) = min_{y >= x} f(y)
    void suffix_min() {
        while (!L.empty()) L.pop();
    }

    // Horizontal shift: expands minimum range by [dL, dR]
    void shift_range(long long dL, long long dR) {
        off_L += dL;
        off_R += dR;
    }

    long long get_min() const {
        return min_f;
    }
};
```

<a id="topic-72"></a>
### 72. What is a Matroid?

> **Section:** §20 Greedy Intermediate (Slope Trick, WQS)  
> **Phase:** Phase 5 — Dynamic Programming & Advanced Greedy  
> **Contest ROI:** A-tier (Provides the ultimate structural proof for when greedy is globally optimal) | **Rating Range:** 2200–2600+ | **Frequency:** Common  

---

#### 1. Contest Payoff & Why It Matters

- Contest ROI: A-tier (Provides the ultimate structural proof for when greedy is globally optimal)
- Typical CF rating range: 2200–2600+
- Mathematical Core: Abstracting linear independence from linear algebra and cycle-freeness from graph theory into a single unified framework.

---

#### 2. Theoretical Deep-Dive & Mathematical Mechanics

##### Overview

Intermediate Greedy techniques bridge convex optimization and dynamic programming: Decoupled Dimension Sweeps, Slope Trick (piecewise linear convex function maintenance via priority queues), and WQS Binary Search (Alien's Trick).

##### Fundamental Theorems & Invariants

- **Additive Independence & Chebyshev Transform:** In $\ell_1$ (Manhattan) and $\ell_\infty$ (Chebyshev) metrics, coordinate transformations $(x', y') = (x+y, x-y)$ decouple multi-dimensional distance minimization into independent 1D median problems.
- **Slope Trick Invariant:** Any continuous piecewise linear convex function $f(x)$ with integer slopes can be represented by its minimum value and two priority queues storing the inflection points where the slope changes by $+1$ and $-1$. Adding $|x - a|$ updates slope inflection points in $\mathcal{O}(\log N)$.
- **Alien's Trick / WQS Binary Search:** If the optimal value $OPT(k)$ as a function of choice count $k$ is strictly concave/convex, introducing a Lagrange multiplier penalty $\lambda$ removes the exact-count constraint, binary searching $\lambda$ in $\mathcal{O}(\text{BaseDP} \cdot \log(\text{Range})))$.

##### Algorithmic Execution Blueprint

1. **Chebyshev Transformation:** Map each point $(x, y) \to (x+y, x-y)$. The maximum Chebyshev distance $\max(|x_1-x_2|, |y_1-y_2|)$ becomes $\frac{1}{2}(|x'_1-x'_2| + |y'_1-y'_2|)$.
2. **Slope Trick Maintenance:** Maintain left heap $L$ (max-heap) and right heap $R$ (min-heap). To add $|x - a|$: if $a < L.\text{top}()$, add cost $L.\text{top}() - a$, push $a$ twice to $L$, and move $L.\text{top}()$ to $R$.
3. **WQS Binary Search:** Binary search penalty $\lambda$. Run unconstrained DP where each selection costs $+\lambda$. If optimal selections count $> k$, increase $\lambda$.

##### Closed-Form Invariant Equations

$$f_{new}(x) = f(x) + |x - a|, \quad \text{WQS}: \min_k \big(dp[k] - \lambda k\big) \iff \text{Lagrangian Duality}$$

##### Structural Edge Cases & Assumptions

- Slope Trick requires the underlying objective function to be strictly convex; non-convex cost functions require general CHT or DP.
- In WQS Binary Search, collinear points (flat segments in the convex hull of $OPT(k)$) require careful tie-breaking (preferring max count or min count) to avoid binary search oscillation.

---

#### 3. Core Intuition & Problem Framing

A **matroid** $M = (E, \mathcal{I})$ consists of a finite **ground set** $E$ and a family of subsets $\mathcal{I} \subseteq 2^E$ called **independent sets**, satisfying:

1. **Non-emptiness:**
   $$\emptyset \in \mathcal{I}$$
2. **Hereditary Property (Downward Closure):**
   $$\text{If } A \in \mathcal{I} \text{ and } B \subseteq A, \text{ then } B \in \mathcal{I}$$
3. **Augmentation / Exchange Property:**
   $$\text{If } A, B \in \mathcal{I} \text{ and } |A| < |B|, \text{ there exists } x \in B \setminus A \text{ such that } A \cup \{x\} \in \mathcal{I}$$

---

#### 6. Recognition Trigger Signals & Heuristics

- Axiom 3 guarantees that any smaller independent set $A$ can always be extended by drawing an element from a larger independent set $B$ without losing independence.** There are no dead-end local optima!

-

<a id="topic-77"></a>
### 77. Partition Matroid

> **Section:** §20 Greedy Intermediate (Slope Trick, WQS)  
> **Phase:** Phase 5 — Dynamic Programming & Advanced Greedy  
> **Contest ROI:** B-tier | **Rating Range:** 1600–2200 | **Frequency:** Common  

---

#### 2. Theoretical Deep-Dive & Mathematical Mechanics

##### Overview

Intermediate Greedy techniques bridge convex optimization and dynamic programming: Decoupled Dimension Sweeps, Slope Trick (piecewise linear convex function maintenance via priority queues), and WQS Binary Search (Alien's Trick).

##### Fundamental Theorems & Invariants

- **Additive Independence & Chebyshev Transform:** In $\ell_1$ (Manhattan) and $\ell_\infty$ (Chebyshev) metrics, coordinate transformations $(x', y') = (x+y, x-y)$ decouple multi-dimensional distance minimization into independent 1D median problems.
- **Slope Trick Invariant:** Any continuous piecewise linear convex function $f(x)$ with integer slopes can be represented by its minimum value and two priority queues storing the inflection points where the slope changes by $+1$ and $-1$. Adding $|x - a|$ updates slope inflection points in $\mathcal{O}(\log N)$.
- **Alien's Trick / WQS Binary Search:** If the optimal value $OPT(k)$ as a function of choice count $k$ is strictly concave/convex, introducing a Lagrange multiplier penalty $\lambda$ removes the exact-count constraint, binary searching $\lambda$ in $\mathcal{O}(\text{BaseDP} \cdot \log(\text{Range})))$.

##### Algorithmic Execution Blueprint

1. **Chebyshev Transformation:** Map each point $(x, y) \to (x+y, x-y)$. The maximum Chebyshev distance $\max(|x_1-x_2|, |y_1-y_2|)$ becomes $\frac{1}{2}(|x'_1-x'_2| + |y'_1-y'_2|)$.
2. **Slope Trick Maintenance:** Maintain left heap $L$ (max-heap) and right heap $R$ (min-heap). To add $|x - a|$: if $a < L.\text{top}()$, add cost $L.\text{top}() - a$, push $a$ twice to $L$, and move $L.\text{top}()$ to $R$.
3. **WQS Binary Search:** Binary search penalty $\lambda$. Run unconstrained DP where each selection costs $+\lambda$. If optimal selections count $> k$, increase $\lambda$.

##### Closed-Form Invariant Equations

$$f_{new}(x) = f(x) + |x - a|, \quad \text{WQS}: \min_k \big(dp[k] - \lambda k\big) \iff \text{Lagrangian Duality}$$

##### Structural Edge Cases & Assumptions

- Slope Trick requires the underlying objective function to be strictly convex; non-convex cost functions require general CHT or DP.
- In WQS Binary Search, collinear points (flat segments in the convex hull of $OPT(k)$) require careful tie-breaking (preferring max count or min count) to avoid binary search oscillation.

---

#### 5. Contest-Ready Implementation Templates

##### C++17 Template

```cpp
// C++17 — Generic Matroid Greedy Engine (XOR Basis Instance)
#include <bits/stdc++.h>
using namespace std;

struct Element {
    long long value;
    long long weight;
    int id;
};

pair<long long, vector<int>> solve_xor_matroid(vector<Element> elements) {
    // 1. Sort descending by weight
    sort(elements.begin(), elements.end(), [](const Element& a, const Element& b) {
        return a.weight > b.weight;
    });

    long long basis[64] = {0};
    long long total_weight = 0;
    vector<int> chosen_ids;

    // 2. Greedy independence oracle check
    for (const auto& elem : elements) {
        long long mask = elem.value;
        for (int b = 62; b >= 0; --b) {
            if ((mask >> b) & 1) {
                if (!basis[b]) {
                    basis[b] = mask;
                    total_weight += elem.weight;
                    chosen_ids.push_back(elem.id);
                    break;
                }
                mask ^= basis[b];
            }
        }
    }
    return {total_weight, chosen_ids};
}
```

<a id="topic-80"></a>
### 80. Why Two Matroids Break Greedy, and How Augmenting Paths Fix It

> **Section:** §20 Greedy Intermediate (Slope Trick, WQS)  
> **Phase:** Phase 5 — Dynamic Programming & Advanced Greedy  
> **Contest ROI:** A-tier (The ultimate tool for multi-constraint combinatorial problems) | **Rating Range:** 2400–2800 | **Frequency:** Common  

---

#### 1. Contest Payoff & Why It Matters

- Contest ROI: A-tier (The ultimate tool for multi-constraint combinatorial problems)
- Typical CF rating range: 2400–2800
- Problem Shape: Find maximum subset $I \subseteq E$ satisfying **two independent matroid constraints** simultaneously ($I \in \mathcal{I}_1$ and $I \in \mathcal{I}_2$).
- Examples: Colorful Spanning Tree (Graphic + Partition), Directed Tree / Arborescence (Graphic + Partition), Maximum Bipartite Matching (Partition + Partition).

---

#### 2. Theoretical Deep-Dive & Mathematical Mechanics

##### Overview

Intermediate Greedy techniques bridge convex optimization and dynamic programming: Decoupled Dimension Sweeps, Slope Trick (piecewise linear convex function maintenance via priority queues), and WQS Binary Search (Alien's Trick).

##### Fundamental Theorems & Invariants

- **Additive Independence & Chebyshev Transform:** In $\ell_1$ (Manhattan) and $\ell_\infty$ (Chebyshev) metrics, coordinate transformations $(x', y') = (x+y, x-y)$ decouple multi-dimensional distance minimization into independent 1D median problems.
- **Slope Trick Invariant:** Any continuous piecewise linear convex function $f(x)$ with integer slopes can be represented by its minimum value and two priority queues storing the inflection points where the slope changes by $+1$ and $-1$. Adding $|x - a|$ updates slope inflection points in $\mathcal{O}(\log N)$.
- **Alien's Trick / WQS Binary Search:** If the optimal value $OPT(k)$ as a function of choice count $k$ is strictly concave/convex, introducing a Lagrange multiplier penalty $\lambda$ removes the exact-count constraint, binary searching $\lambda$ in $\mathcal{O}(\text{BaseDP} \cdot \log(\text{Range})))$.

##### Algorithmic Execution Blueprint

1. **Chebyshev Transformation:** Map each point $(x, y) \to (x+y, x-y)$. The maximum Chebyshev distance $\max(|x_1-x_2|, |y_1-y_2|)$ becomes $\frac{1}{2}(|x'_1-x'_2| + |y'_1-y'_2|)$.
2. **Slope Trick Maintenance:** Maintain left heap $L$ (max-heap) and right heap $R$ (min-heap). To add $|x - a|$: if $a < L.\text{top}()$, add cost $L.\text{top}() - a$, push $a$ twice to $L$, and move $L.\text{top}()$ to $R$.
3. **WQS Binary Search:** Binary search penalty $\lambda$. Run unconstrained DP where each selection costs $+\lambda$. If optimal selections count $> k$, increase $\lambda$.

##### Closed-Form Invariant Equations

$$f_{new}(x) = f(x) + |x - a|, \quad \text{WQS}: \min_k \big(dp[k] - \lambda k\big) \iff \text{Lagrangian Duality}$$

##### Structural Edge Cases & Assumptions

- Slope Trick requires the underlying objective function to be strictly convex; non-convex cost functions require general CHT or DP.
- In WQS Binary Search, collinear points (flat segments in the convex hull of $OPT(k)$) require careful tie-breaking (preferring max count or min count) to avoid binary search oscillation.

---

#### 3. Core Intuition & Problem Framing

Although the intersection of two matroids satisfies Axioms 1 and 2 (hereditary), it **violates Axiom 3 (the exchange property)**. A single greedy pass can make a locally optimal choice that irreversibly blocks simultaneous independence in both structures.
To solve this in polynomial time, we use Edmonds' **Augmenting Path Algorithm** on an **Exchange Graph**.

---

## 2. The Exchange Graph $G_I$ & The Augmentation Algorithm

Given a currently valid independent set $I \in \mathcal{I}_1 \cap \mathcal{I}_2$, construct a directed bipartite graph $G_I(V, A)$ where $V = E$:
- Elements **inside** $I$: $x \in I$
- Elements **outside** $I$: $y \notin I$

```
   Sources (X1)                      Sinks (X2)
  [ y in E \ I ]                    [ y in E \ I ]
  I + y in I_1                      I + y in I_2
        \                                 ^
         \ (M1 swap: y -> x)             / (M2 swap: x -> y)
          v                             /
       [          x in I (Internal)        ]
```

---

<a id="topic-83"></a>
### 83. The Augmentation Step

> **Section:** §20 Greedy Intermediate (Slope Trick, WQS)  
> **Phase:** Phase 5 — Dynamic Programming & Advanced Greedy  
> **Contest ROI:** B-tier | **Rating Range:** 1600–2200 | **Frequency:** Common  

---

#### 2. Theoretical Deep-Dive & Mathematical Mechanics

##### Overview

Intermediate Greedy techniques bridge convex optimization and dynamic programming: Decoupled Dimension Sweeps, Slope Trick (piecewise linear convex function maintenance via priority queues), and WQS Binary Search (Alien's Trick).

##### Fundamental Theorems & Invariants

- **Additive Independence & Chebyshev Transform:** In $\ell_1$ (Manhattan) and $\ell_\infty$ (Chebyshev) metrics, coordinate transformations $(x', y') = (x+y, x-y)$ decouple multi-dimensional distance minimization into independent 1D median problems.
- **Slope Trick Invariant:** Any continuous piecewise linear convex function $f(x)$ with integer slopes can be represented by its minimum value and two priority queues storing the inflection points where the slope changes by $+1$ and $-1$. Adding $|x - a|$ updates slope inflection points in $\mathcal{O}(\log N)$.
- **Alien's Trick / WQS Binary Search:** If the optimal value $OPT(k)$ as a function of choice count $k$ is strictly concave/convex, introducing a Lagrange multiplier penalty $\lambda$ removes the exact-count constraint, binary searching $\lambda$ in $\mathcal{O}(\text{BaseDP} \cdot \log(\text{Range})))$.

##### Algorithmic Execution Blueprint

1. **Chebyshev Transformation:** Map each point $(x, y) \to (x+y, x-y)$. The maximum Chebyshev distance $\max(|x_1-x_2|, |y_1-y_2|)$ becomes $\frac{1}{2}(|x'_1-x'_2| + |y'_1-y'_2|)$.
2. **Slope Trick Maintenance:** Maintain left heap $L$ (max-heap) and right heap $R$ (min-heap). To add $|x - a|$: if $a < L.\text{top}()$, add cost $L.\text{top}() - a$, push $a$ twice to $L$, and move $L.\text{top}()$ to $R$.
3. **WQS Binary Search:** Binary search penalty $\lambda$. Run unconstrained DP where each selection costs $+\lambda$. If optimal selections count $> k$, increase $\lambda$.

##### Closed-Form Invariant Equations

$$f_{new}(x) = f(x) + |x - a|, \quad \text{WQS}: \min_k \big(dp[k] - \lambda k\big) \iff \text{Lagrangian Duality}$$

##### Structural Edge Cases & Assumptions

- Slope Trick requires the underlying objective function to be strictly convex; non-convex cost functions require general CHT or DP.
- In WQS Binary Search, collinear points (flat segments in the convex hull of $OPT(k)$) require careful tie-breaking (preferring max count or min count) to avoid binary search oscillation.

---

#### 5. Contest-Ready Implementation Templates

##### C++17 Template

```cpp
// C++17 — Generic Matroid Intersection Engine
#include <bits/stdc++.h>
using namespace std;

template <typename Matroid1, typename Matroid2>
vector<int> matroid_intersection(int n, Matroid1& m1, Matroid2& m2) {
    vector<int> I;
    vector<bool> in_I(n, false);

    while (true) {
        vector<int> X1, X2;
        vector<vector<int>> adj(n);

        // 1. Identify Sources (X1) and Sinks (X2)
        for (int y = 0; y < n; ++y) {
            if (in_I[y]) continue;
            if (m1.can_add(I, y)) X1.push_back(y);
            if (m2.can_add(I, y)) X2.push_back(y);
        }

        // 2. Build Exchange Graph Edges
        for (int x : I) {
            vector<int> I_minus_x;
            for (int elem : I) if (elem != x) I_minus_x.push_back(elem);

            for (int y = 0; y < n; ++y) {
                if (in_I[y]) continue;
                // M1 edge: y -> x
                if (m1.can_add(I_minus_x, y)) adj[y].push_back(x);
                // M2 edge: x -> y
                if (m2.can_add(I_minus_x, y)) adj[x].push_back(y);
            }
        }

        // 3. BFS Shortest Path from X1 to X2
        vector<int> parent(n, -1);
        queue<int> q;
        for (int src : X1) {
            q.push(src);
            parent[src] = src;
        }

        int target = -1;
        vector<bool> is_sink(n, false);
        for (int snk : X2) is_sink[snk] = true;

        while (!q.empty() && target == -1) {
            int u = q.front(); q.pop();
            if (is_sink[u]) {
                target = u;
                break;
            }
            for (int v : adj[u]) {
                if (parent[v] == -1) {
                    parent[v] = u;
                    q.push(v);
                }
            }
        }

        if (target == -1) break; // Maximum independent set reached

        // 4. Augment along the shortest path
        int curr = target;
        while (true) {
            in_I[curr] = !in_I[curr];
            if (curr == parent[curr]) break;
            curr = parent[curr];
        }

        I.clear();
        for (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
            if (in_I[i]) I.push_back(i);
        }
    }
    return I;
}
```

<a id="topic-87"></a>
### 87. The MSB Dominance Principle in Bitwise Optimization

> **Section:** §20 Greedy Intermediate (Slope Trick, WQS)  
> **Phase:** Phase 5 — Dynamic Programming & Advanced Greedy  
> **Contest ROI:** S-tier (Essential in Codeforces 1800–2300) | **Rating Range:** 1600–2200 | **Frequency:** Common  

---

#### 1. Contest Payoff & Why It Matters

- Contest ROI: S-tier (Essential in Codeforces 1800–2300)
- Core Mathematical Property: Strict dominance of powers of two:
  $$2^k > \sum_{i=0}^{k-1} 2^i = 2^k - 1$$
- Implication: A greedy decision securing a `1` at the $k$-th bit is **unconditionally superior** to any combination of choices made across all lower bits $0 \dots k-1$. No backtracking or DP is ever required!

---

#### 2. Theoretical Deep-Dive & Mathematical Mechanics

##### Overview

Intermediate Greedy techniques bridge convex optimization and dynamic programming: Decoupled Dimension Sweeps, Slope Trick (piecewise linear convex function maintenance via priority queues), and WQS Binary Search (Alien's Trick).

##### Fundamental Theorems & Invariants

- **Additive Independence & Chebyshev Transform:** In $\ell_1$ (Manhattan) and $\ell_\infty$ (Chebyshev) metrics, coordinate transformations $(x', y') = (x+y, x-y)$ decouple multi-dimensional distance minimization into independent 1D median problems.
- **Slope Trick Invariant:** Any continuous piecewise linear convex function $f(x)$ with integer slopes can be represented by its minimum value and two priority queues storing the inflection points where the slope changes by $+1$ and $-1$. Adding $|x - a|$ updates slope inflection points in $\mathcal{O}(\log N)$.
- **Alien's Trick / WQS Binary Search:** If the optimal value $OPT(k)$ as a function of choice count $k$ is strictly concave/convex, introducing a Lagrange multiplier penalty $\lambda$ removes the exact-count constraint, binary searching $\lambda$ in $\mathcal{O}(\text{BaseDP} \cdot \log(\text{Range})))$.

##### Algorithmic Execution Blueprint

1. **Chebyshev Transformation:** Map each point $(x, y) \to (x+y, x-y)$. The maximum Chebyshev distance $\max(|x_1-x_2|, |y_1-y_2|)$ becomes $\frac{1}{2}(|x'_1-x'_2| + |y'_1-y'_2|)$.
2. **Slope Trick Maintenance:** Maintain left heap $L$ (max-heap) and right heap $R$ (min-heap). To add $|x - a|$: if $a < L.\text{top}()$, add cost $L.\text{top}() - a$, push $a$ twice to $L$, and move $L.\text{top}()$ to $R$.
3. **WQS Binary Search:** Binary search penalty $\lambda$. Run unconstrained DP where each selection costs $+\lambda$. If optimal selections count $> k$, increase $\lambda$.

##### Closed-Form Invariant Equations

$$f_{new}(x) = f(x) + |x - a|, \quad \text{WQS}: \min_k \big(dp[k] - \lambda k\big) \iff \text{Lagrangian Duality}$$

##### Structural Edge Cases & Assumptions

- Slope Trick requires the underlying objective function to be strictly convex; non-convex cost functions require general CHT or DP.
- In WQS Binary Search, collinear points (flat segments in the convex hull of $OPT(k)$) require careful tie-breaking (preferring max count or min count) to avoid binary search oscillation.

---

#### 3. Core Intuition & Problem Framing

1. Build a Binary Trie holding bit representations of elements from MSB (bit 29) down to LSB (bit 0).
2. For query $X$, at bit $k$, let $b = (X \gg k) \& 1$.
3. The greedy target bit is $b \oplus 1$. If a branch exists with bit $b \oplus 1$, take it; otherwise, take $b$.
4. **Max XOR Subarray:** Use running prefix XOR $P[i] = A[0] \oplus \dots \oplus A[i]$. Since $\text{XOR}(A[L \dots R]) = P[R] \oplus P[L-1]$, insert each $P[i]$ into the Trie and query for max XOR with $P[i]$.

```cpp
// C++17 — High-Performance Flat Array Binary Trie
#include <bits/stdc++.h>
using namespace std;

class BinaryTrie {
    static const int BITS = 30;
    vector<array<int, 2>> trie;
    vector<int> sz;

public:
    BinaryTrie(int max_nodes = 3000000) {
        trie.reserve(max_nodes);
        sz.reserve(max_nodes);
        trie.push_back({0, 0});
        sz.push_back(0);
    }

    void insert(int val) {
        int u = 0;
        sz[u]++;
        for (int i = BITS - 1; i >= 0; --i) {
            int b = (val >> i) & 1;
            if (!trie[u][b]) {
                trie[u][b] = trie.size();
                trie.push_back({0, 0});
                sz.push_back(0);
            }
            u = trie[u][b];
            sz[u]++;
        }
    }

    int query_max_xor(int val) {
        int u = 0, res = 0;
        for (int i = BITS - 1; i >= 0; --i) {
            int b = (val >> i) & 1;
            int desired = b ^ 1;
            if (trie[u][desired] && sz[trie[u][desired]] > 0) {
                res |= (1 << i);
                u = trie[u][desired];
            } else {
                u = trie[u][b];
            }
        }
        return res;
    }
};
```

---

## 2. Segment Tree In-Tree Walking

---

<a id="topic-88"></a>
### 88. Single-Pass $O(\log N)$ Binary Search on SegTrees

> **Section:** §20 Greedy Intermediate (Slope Trick, WQS)  
> **Phase:** Phase 5 — Dynamic Programming & Advanced Greedy  
> **Contest ROI:** B-tier | **Rating Range:** 1600–2200 | **Frequency:** Common  

---

#### 1. Contest Payoff & Why It Matters

- Replaces external binary search ($O(\log^2 N)$) with a direct in-tree descent in $O(\log N)$.
- Crucial for dynamic resource allocation and finding first/last indices satisfying bounds under point/range updates.

---

#### 2. Theoretical Deep-Dive & Mathematical Mechanics

##### Overview

Intermediate Greedy techniques bridge convex optimization and dynamic programming: Decoupled Dimension Sweeps, Slope Trick (piecewise linear convex function maintenance via priority queues), and WQS Binary Search (Alien's Trick).

##### Fundamental Theorems & Invariants

- **Additive Independence & Chebyshev Transform:** In $\ell_1$ (Manhattan) and $\ell_\infty$ (Chebyshev) metrics, coordinate transformations $(x', y') = (x+y, x-y)$ decouple multi-dimensional distance minimization into independent 1D median problems.
- **Slope Trick Invariant:** Any continuous piecewise linear convex function $f(x)$ with integer slopes can be represented by its minimum value and two priority queues storing the inflection points where the slope changes by $+1$ and $-1$. Adding $|x - a|$ updates slope inflection points in $\mathcal{O}(\log N)$.
- **Alien's Trick / WQS Binary Search:** If the optimal value $OPT(k)$ as a function of choice count $k$ is strictly concave/convex, introducing a Lagrange multiplier penalty $\lambda$ removes the exact-count constraint, binary searching $\lambda$ in $\mathcal{O}(\text{BaseDP} \cdot \log(\text{Range})))$.

##### Algorithmic Execution Blueprint

1. **Chebyshev Transformation:** Map each point $(x, y) \to (x+y, x-y)$. The maximum Chebyshev distance $\max(|x_1-x_2|, |y_1-y_2|)$ becomes $\frac{1}{2}(|x'_1-x'_2| + |y'_1-y'_2|)$.
2. **Slope Trick Maintenance:** Maintain left heap $L$ (max-heap) and right heap $R$ (min-heap). To add $|x - a|$: if $a < L.\text{top}()$, add cost $L.\text{top}() - a$, push $a$ twice to $L$, and move $L.\text{top}()$ to $R$.
3. **WQS Binary Search:** Binary search penalty $\lambda$. Run unconstrained DP where each selection costs $+\lambda$. If optimal selections count $> k$, increase $\lambda$.

##### Closed-Form Invariant Equations

$$f_{new}(x) = f(x) + |x - a|, \quad \text{WQS}: \min_k \big(dp[k] - \lambda k\big) \iff \text{Lagrangian Duality}$$

##### Structural Edge Cases & Assumptions

- Slope Trick requires the underlying objective function to be strictly convex; non-convex cost functions require general CHT or DP.
- In WQS Binary Search, collinear points (flat segments in the convex hull of $OPT(k)$) require careful tie-breaking (preferring max count or min count) to avoid binary search oscillation.

---

#### 3. Core Intuition & Problem Framing

To find the first index $i \ge L$ such that $A[i] \ge X$:
1. If the current node's range $[tl, tr]$ lies outside $[L, R]$ or $\max(node) < X$, prune immediately ($O(1)$).
2. If at a leaf, return its index.
3. Recursively search the **left child** first. If it yields a valid index, return it immediately without exploring the right child.
4. Otherwise, search the **right child**.

```cpp
// C++17 — Walkable Segment Tree (Find First >= X starting from L)
#include <bits/stdc++.h>
using namespace std;

struct WalkableSegTree {
    int n;
    vector<int> tree;

    WalkableSegTree(int n, const vector<int>& a) : n(n), tree(4 * n) {
        build(1, 0, n - 1, a);
    }

    void build(int v, int tl, int tr, const vector<int>& a) {
        if (tl == tr) tree[v] = a[tl];
        else {
            int tm = (tl + tr) / 2;
            build(v * 2, tl, tm, a);
            build(v * 2 + 1, tm + 1, tr, a);
            tree[v] = max(tree[v * 2], tree[v * 2 + 1]);
        }
    }

    void update(int v, int tl, int tr, int pos, int val) {
        if (tl == tr) tree[v] = val;
        else {
            int tm = (tl + tr) / 2;
            if (pos <= tm) update(v * 2, tl, tm, pos, val);
            else update(v * 2 + 1, tm + 1, tr, pos, val);
            tree[v] = max(tree[v * 2], tree[v * 2 + 1]);
        }
    }

    int find_first(int v, int tl, int tr, int l, int r, int x) {
        if (tl > r || tr < l || tree[v] < x) return -1;
        if (tl == tr) return tl;
        int tm = (tl + tr) / 2;
        int res = find_first(v * 2, tl, tm, l, r, x);
        if (res != -1) return res;
        return find_first(v * 2 + 1, tm + 1, tr, l, r, x);
    }
};
```

---

## 3. Hall’s Marriage Theorem & Lazy SegTree Oracles

---

#### 5. Contest-Ready Implementation Templates

##### C++17 Template

```cpp
// C++17 — Walkable Segment Tree (Find First >= X starting from L)
#include <bits/stdc++.h>
using namespace std;

struct WalkableSegTree {
    int n;
    vector<int> tree;

    WalkableSegTree(int n, const vector<int>& a) : n(n), tree(4 * n) {
        build(1, 0, n - 1, a);
    }

    void build(int v, int tl, int tr, const vector<int>& a) {
        if (tl == tr) tree[v] = a[tl];
        else {
            int tm = (tl + tr) / 2;
            build(v * 2, tl, tm, a);
            build(v * 2 + 1, tm + 1, tr, a);
            tree[v] = max(tree[v * 2], tree[v * 2 + 1]);
        }
    }

    void update(int v, int tl, int tr, int pos, int val) {
        if (tl == tr) tree[v] = val;
        else {
            int tm = (tl + tr) / 2;
            if (pos <= tm) update(v * 2, tl, tm, pos, val);
            else update(v * 2 + 1, tm + 1, tr, pos, val);
            tree[v] = max(tree[v * 2], tree[v * 2 + 1]);
        }
    }

    int find_first(int v, int tl, int tr, int l, int r, int x) {
        if (tl > r || tr < l || tree[v] < x) return -1;
        if (tl == tr) return tl;
        int tm = (tl + tr) / 2;
        int res = find_first(v * 2, tl, tm, l, r, x);
        if (res != -1) return res;
        return find_first(v * 2 + 1, tm + 1, tr, l, r, x);
    }
};
```

<a id="topic-89"></a>
### 89. Dynamic Verification of Bipartite Matchability

> **Section:** §20 Greedy Intermediate (Slope Trick, WQS)  
> **Phase:** Phase 5 — Dynamic Programming & Advanced Greedy  
> **Contest ROI:** B-tier | **Rating Range:** 1600–2200 | **Frequency:** Common  

---

#### 1. Contest Payoff & Why It Matters

In a bipartite graph $G(L \cup R, E)$, a matching covering all $L$ exists **if and only if** for every $S \subseteq L$:
$$|N(S)| \ge |S|$$

---

#### 2. Theoretical Deep-Dive & Mathematical Mechanics

##### Overview

Intermediate Greedy techniques bridge convex optimization and dynamic programming: Decoupled Dimension Sweeps, Slope Trick (piecewise linear convex function maintenance via priority queues), and WQS Binary Search (Alien's Trick).

##### Fundamental Theorems & Invariants

- **Additive Independence & Chebyshev Transform:** In $\ell_1$ (Manhattan) and $\ell_\infty$ (Chebyshev) metrics, coordinate transformations $(x', y') = (x+y, x-y)$ decouple multi-dimensional distance minimization into independent 1D median problems.
- **Slope Trick Invariant:** Any continuous piecewise linear convex function $f(x)$ with integer slopes can be represented by its minimum value and two priority queues storing the inflection points where the slope changes by $+1$ and $-1$. Adding $|x - a|$ updates slope inflection points in $\mathcal{O}(\log N)$.
- **Alien's Trick / WQS Binary Search:** If the optimal value $OPT(k)$ as a function of choice count $k$ is strictly concave/convex, introducing a Lagrange multiplier penalty $\lambda$ removes the exact-count constraint, binary searching $\lambda$ in $\mathcal{O}(\text{BaseDP} \cdot \log(\text{Range})))$.

##### Algorithmic Execution Blueprint

1. **Chebyshev Transformation:** Map each point $(x, y) \to (x+y, x-y)$. The maximum Chebyshev distance $\max(|x_1-x_2|, |y_1-y_2|)$ becomes $\frac{1}{2}(|x'_1-x'_2| + |y'_1-y'_2|)$.
2. **Slope Trick Maintenance:** Maintain left heap $L$ (max-heap) and right heap $R$ (min-heap). To add $|x - a|$: if $a < L.\text{top}()$, add cost $L.\text{top}() - a$, push $a$ twice to $L$, and move $L.\text{top}()$ to $R$.
3. **WQS Binary Search:** Binary search penalty $\lambda$. Run unconstrained DP where each selection costs $+\lambda$. If optimal selections count $> k$, increase $\lambda$.

##### Closed-Form Invariant Equations

$$f_{new}(x) = f(x) + |x - a|, \quad \text{WQS}: \min_k \big(dp[k] - \lambda k\big) \iff \text{Lagrangian Duality}$$

##### Structural Edge Cases & Assumptions

- Slope Trick requires the underlying objective function to be strictly convex; non-convex cost functions require general CHT or DP.
- In WQS Binary Search, collinear points (flat segments in the convex hull of $OPT(k)$) require careful tie-breaking (preferring max count or min count) to avoid binary search oscillation.

---

#### 3. Core Intuition & Problem Framing

When $R$ represents an ordered sequence and elements of $L$ request intervals $[l_i, r_i]$, Hall's condition reduces to checking all contiguous subsegments $[x, y]$:
$$\min_{x \le y} \big( y - x + 1 - \text{count}(x, y) \big) \ge 0$$
Using a Lazy Segment Tree, we maintain the "slack" array $D[x] = x$. When an item requesting $[l_i, r_i]$ is assigned, it subtracts 1 from the range $[0 \dots l_i]$. If the global minimum slack of the tree stays $\ge 0$, the greedy choice is globally safe and extendable!

---
